# Changelog

Versions are listed newest first. Entries are concise summaries of notable outcomes; the version-session rules and allowed labels are defined in `AGENTS.md`.

## 0.9.28 — 2026-09-19

- [Fixed] The two switches this version adds no longer live in the file that carries the registered Cheat Engine. That file is parsed strictly, so a build that does not know a field refuses the whole of it: a Steam Deck updating from this version to the published one came back saying Cheat Engine was not installed, while the installation and its registration sat untouched on disk. They have their own file now, which ignores a key it does not know and reads as the defaults when it cannot be read at all, because nothing in it authorizes anything. A configuration that already carries them has them moved out of it once, at load, and the user's choices come with them.
- [Fixed] A search now asks whether there is an update instead of waiting for the next timer tick. What arms the check is somebody using the plugin, and the check itself happened only when the timer next came round: measured on a Steam Deck, the tick landed twenty-four seconds before the search, so the device that had just been searched on waited another five minutes before asking, and the panel said nothing about an update that was one request away. Everything that decides whether a check happens at all is unchanged, and the search's own answer is never held up for it.
- [Fixed] The update button on the panel could not be reached with a controller, and sat against the left edge. It was a bare box between the mascot and the first section, and the quick access menu walks Steam's own section rows: the one press this version adds to the panel was therefore visible and unusable in Game Mode, which is the only mode it exists for. It is a full-width press inside a section of its own now, like every other action on that panel. Its orange also stopped Steam painting it as focused, because an inline background outranks the class Steam paints with; the colour is a rule that stands aside while anything inside it holds the ring.
- [Fixed] A search now asks whether there is an update instead of waiting for the next timer tick. What arms the check is somebody using the plugin, and the check itself happened only when the timer next came round: measured on a Steam Deck, the tick landed twenty-four seconds before the search, so the device that had just been searched on waited another five minutes before asking, and the panel said nothing about an update that was one request away. Everything that decides whether a check happens at all is unchanged, and the search's own answer is never held up for it.
- [Fixed] A second review round over this version. Installing refuses the way out because the interface restart is what closes that window, which left no exit at all when the restart was the thing that failed; after a wait longer than a restart ever takes, the way out comes back. A check that reached GitHub reported failure when the file it writes its answer to could not be written. An install that landed could be reported as failed, because Decky can accept the request and the installer can still fall over reading the answer, which sent the user to install by hand a version their device was already running. Advanced offered an install on a device that cannot carry one out, and its own switch and two documents promised that switching automatic checking off means nothing contacts GitHub, while Check now still asks when pressed. And the mascot was drawn for a moment on every panel rebuild in the face of the user who had just switched it off.
- [Fixed] A review pass over this version's own work: a download that failed left a partial archive in the managed tree, a failed install named whatever an earlier check had found, Check now was paced against the background timer as well as against presses, and the token that authorizes installing a plugin was requested from whatever URL the caller named before anything checked it was loopback. Deleting plugin data did not refuse while an update was in flight, the panel offered an update on a device that cannot install one, and the one press this version adds to the panel carried no test id, so the device's own panel reader could not see it at all.
- [Changed] The installer and the package probe can be told which version a package should carry. They prove a ZIP is the exact installable artifact of this checkout, and the build that exercises the plugin's own update path is deliberately older than the release it installs, so it was refused. Naming the version moves what the package is compared against and nothing else: a package or a checkout whose own two version strings disagree still fails.
- [Fixed] One call to the live backend through the development RPC helper could print another call's answer. Decky delivers a plugin method's reply to sockets that did not ask for it, and every call drew the same request id, so a broadcast of an earlier reply was indistinguishable from this call's own: two identical calls seconds apart printed one answer twice while the backend's journal recorded one call. Each call draws its own id now, and docs/FIELD_NOTES.md carries the behaviour it exists for.
- [New] CE Decky notices its own new releases and installs one on an explicit press. When an update is available the panel carries an orange Update to vX.Y.Z button above everything else, and pressing it says which version replaces which, that Steam's interface restarts, and that installing cannot be cancelled once it starts. The release is downloaded, checked against the checksum the release itself publishes, and handed to Decky's own installer, so nothing here needs root and nothing replaces the plugin directory by hand. Advanced, Plugin updates has the same press, a Check now, and the switch that turns the whole thing off; with it off nothing contacts GitHub and the panel offers nothing. Automatic checking is armed by use rather than by a clock: it asks only after you have searched for a table recently, at most once every few hours, which is how the table listing already decides whether it owes itself a refresh.
- [New] The panel mascot can be switched off. Advanced, Panel appearance carries the switch, it is on by default, and the choice lives beside the Cheat Engine registration in the plugin's own settings, so it survives an update and a reinstall. Switched off, the image is absent rather than hidden: the height it took goes back to the cheats.

## 0.9.27 — 2026-09-14

- [Fixed] Rebuilding the committed runtime dependencies no longer depends on which interpreter started the command. SteamOS ships a system python3 with no pip, so running the documented command the documented way produced a pip traceback out of a failed subprocess. It borrows the development environment this project already caches when the interpreter it was started by cannot install, and says which command creates that environment when there is none.
- [Fixed] A component test raced the data it asserted. It awaited the pager footer and then read the preferred-child position that only the fetched section index decides, so it passed on a fast machine and failed on a loaded CI runner. It waits for the answer now rather than for the element the answer appears in, and that third shape is recorded beside the two the development document already carried.
- [Changed] The operating contract is worked from rather than read around. It is a file every clean run reads whole, and two fifths of it sat in blocks of ninety words or more where a rule and its reason and the story of the incident that produced it ran together. The rules are unchanged and each one was checked for after the edit; what went is the narration, one statement of the same claim in three places, and a restatement of an install procedure the development document already owns. Four things it said were no longer true, among them a section pointing at a pass that has happened, and the instruction not to wait for CI, which contradicted the sentence above it and the release rule below it.
- [Changed] The UI action audit is a standing contract rather than a dated one. It was titled for a version five behind, opened on a source baseline commit, and grouped its guarantees by the four follow-up commits that introduced them, and none of those five commits exists in this repository, so every pin a reader could check was unresolvable. The guarantees are grouped by subject now, with the regressions that hold them, and the sentence about what the audit closed under a field-notes section that is empty is gone.
- [Changed] The trust contract reads one source at a time. Five provider adapters were three paragraphs inside the section about local file integrity, which is not what they are about, so what any single source is held to could only be found by reading past the others. They are bullets under their own names now, in a provider adapter boundary section of their own, and the controls that hold for every source alike stayed shared rather than moving inside one provider's entry.
- [Changed] A helper now has to be findable, not merely documented. The rule that held helper documentation read the development document only, so eleven helpers satisfied it while none of them appeared in the tables AGENTS.md sends an agent to before writing a command of its own, and the gate stayed green throughout. A second rule reads those two tables, prose elsewhere in the contract deliberately does not count, and a helper that stays out carries its reason in a list rather than in somebody's memory. The documentation rule also covers tools/ now, which had no enforcement at all.
- [Changed] The tracked-helper table answers the questions it was missing. Eight helpers were reachable only from the development document: whether Cheat Engine actually starts through the production launch path, which prefix an AppID has, what Steam's own JavaScript says the library holds, whether the device's 7-Zip opens what the adapter hands it, whether a provider answers over TLS, whether an archive is what the Store would ship, what to do when the runtime lock moves, and how a published screenshot strip is assembled. A question with no row is where an agent writes the shell command the table exists to replace.
- [Changed] The document about driving a device over SSH describes the tools that drive it. It carried a blanket refusal of synthetic input written before the panel reader could open a screen, and named none of the three helpers that reach a device: reading this plugin's own rows and pressing its own navigation controls, capturing the other machine's screen, and calling the live backend. The refusal stays, narrowed to what it is actually about, which is fabricated controller input and Steam's own interface.
- [Fixed] Two library tests proved a property of the machine they ran on rather than of the reader. A shortcut pointing at an absolute path that is not on the device is treated as absent, and the fixtures pointed at the host's own flatpak wrapper, so the case about how a shortcut was made never ran on a machine without flatpak and the suite was red there. The wrapper is created by the test now, and the case that adapted itself to the host no longer does.
- [Fixed] The release archive is the same bytes from any checkout of its commit. The packager copied each file's filesystem permissions into the ZIP, and Git records only one bit of them, so a member left at 0600 in one working tree and 0644 in another produced two different SHA-256 for identical content. Modes are normalized now, keeping the executable bit Git does record, which is what makes a release attestation something a reader can reproduce.
- [Changed] The publication checklist is gone, because publication happened. What it knew that outlives it was moved first: the Store build contract that was read from upstream rather than remembered, the procedure for moving the pinned Cheat Engine release and the tools that serve it, and the reason the generative-AI attestation stays unchecked. The managed Cheat Engine provenance it wanted written down was already in the security contract.
- [Fixed] The archive the official Decky Store builds carries the Python packages the backend imports. That build copies py_modules as the repository holds it and installs nothing, so the vendor tree this project produced while packaging reached its own ZIP and never reached the Store one, whose backend died on its first import. The tree is committed now, scripts/update_runtime_vendor.py rebuilds it from the hash-pinned lock, and a lock bumped without a rebuild or a tree edited after one is refused before packaging.
- [Fixed] The third-party notices and license texts reach that archive too. The Decky CLI packages a fixed set of top-level files, so both sat at the root where only this project's own packager could see them; they live under defaults/ now, which is the one prefix that build strips, and both archives carry them at the same paths.
- [New] plugin.json carries the publish block the Store reads for its listing, under a maintainer identity a reviewer can contact. Nothing in that pipeline minds when it is absent: the archive builds, the upload succeeds, and the card is simply blank.
- [New] scripts/check_store_artifact.py builds the Store archive with the pinned Decky CLI and checks what a submission would ship, and CI runs it beside the release gate. python scripts/qa.py --profile store is the same check as a stage, and it reports INCOMPLETE where no container engine exists.

## 0.9.26 — 2026-09-14

- [Changed] `docs/FIELD_NOTES.md` **Still worth checking** is empty, which is the condition a stable tag is refused on. Auto-load disarming itself is settled: switching off the last cheat with nothing running switches auto-load off with it, the enclosing scripts this plugin manages on the reader's behalf never reach the saved selection, and the picker opened again seeds every row off and turns on only what the table was configured to do - three claims, each now held by a regression rather than by a row asking someone to try it. The large-dropdown question is answered by a bound that predates the row: a record is offered at most 24 values at once whatever it declares, so Steam is never handed a large list, and `docs/DESIGN.md` states that rather than the note. The survey of sources in other languages is dropped as work this project is not doing.
- [Fixed] A screen refits for every block it can mount on its own rather than for one of them. The cheat picker puts two under its list - a refusal it reports, and the acknowledgement it asks for before closing over unapplied edits - and only the first was watched, so the second could still push the footer behind Steam's bar on a page that had already fitted. Each screen now composes what it can mount into one key, because a flag per block is a flag somebody forgets to pass.
- [Fixed] The publication guard reads its own two files like every other file, and generates a removed-name row from one place. It used to leave itself and its fixture out of the scan, which put the only blind spot in the two files least able to afford one; neither spells a sample out as one string now, so nothing has to be allowed by name to make that work. The row a maintainer pastes in comes from `verify_repo.py --removed-name`, which normalizes exactly as the matcher does - worked out by hand, a Title Cased name produced a row that never matched anything and reported nothing wrong.
- [Fixed] A game whose only Windows program sits deeper than a candidate is looked for is no longer called a native Linux build. Three levels is where a game's own executable is, and that answers which program to offer; it cannot answer whether the install holds one at all, and reading the first as the second is what could refuse the press on a game that has one. Where the candidate search comes back empty the same walk now carries on past that depth, collecting nothing and stopping at the first `.exe`, and only a search that met no bound and no unreadable folder turns an empty answer into a claim. The extra walk is paid for only when Steam's own record and the shallow search have both come back with nothing.
- [Fixed] A screen that has already fitted itself fits again when something else appears on it. These windows report a failed press on a row of their own, which moves the footer without changing its identity or its size - all a fitted page was watching - so a page that fitted went behind Steam's bar and stayed there, which is the exact class the fitter exists to remove. A block appearing or going away is now a new layout, and a new layout gets the correction budget rather than the remains of the one before it, so the row comes back when the reader has dealt with the failure.
- [Fixed] A folder that could not be read is never reported as proof that a game holds no Windows program. That one answer is what the Approve screen acts on: it hides the process picker and refuses the press, on the ground that there is nothing here to attach to, so a directory this may not open - which could have held the one executable - must not produce it. Such a walk now says what it is instead. The depth this stops at is unchanged and is not a hole in the claim: it is where a game's own executable is, and every executable on this machine's own installed games sits well inside it.
- [Fixed] An installed app Steam types `Game` is offered whatever else its record says. The game filter asks two questions and was asking them in the wrong order: an app declaring nothing to start was dropped before the type was read at all, so a game whose record carries no launch map - unusual, but the user's game - disappeared from the picker rather than being offered and refused later with a reason. The type is read first and answers on its own for a `Game`, which is what both the code's own contract and `docs/DESIGN.md` already said.
- [Fixed] Auto-load wakes when the program a table is for finally appears. A game that starts through a launcher of its own holds `Launcher.exe` first and the game itself seconds later, with Steam reporting the game as running throughout; Auto-load rightly declines to launch against a target it has proven absent, and then had nothing to wake it, because what it watched was whether the game was running and that never changed. The automatic workflow stayed asleep for the rest of the session while the manual press beside it worked. It now watches whether the target is proven absent, which is one bit and changes exactly once, rather than the observation itself, which is new on every poll.
- [Changed] The games and tables this plugin was developed against are out of the tree, including the shipped bundle, where two of them sat in source comments. Test fixtures and the one provider-scoring example now use stand-ins, and a Steam account id that had been copied into a fixture from a real device is gone. What stays is a public catalog fact: the identity scorer is tested against real titles because telling one sequel from another is its job, and Steam typing a game's episodes as a tool is why the game filter reads the launch entries beside the type.
- [New] `scripts/verify_repo.py` refuses what a published tree must not carry, which was the last thing the public-release cleanup owed it. An address on somebody's own network, an account on a named device, a Steam user's own directory, the filename a table was downloaded as, a capture kept from a device session, an agent attribution trailer and a payload file all have a shape and are found by it. A game's title has none - nothing separates one from `Steam Deck` without a list of titles - so the guard does not pretend to recognise a name it was never told about, and instead makes a removal stick: a title taken out on purpose is listed as a digest of itself and every one to three word phrase in the tree, whatever its case, is hashed against that list, so the guard catches the name coming back without ever restating it. Every file this project owns is read rather than four source suffixes, because a string moved into a manifest, a workflow or a page of test HTML is published exactly as far as one in a source file.
- [Changed] `docs/FIELD_NOTES.md` **Still worth checking** holds three questions rather than eight. Visible-game controller focus after a Cheat Engine interaction is settled; choosing another game while an owned Cheat Engine holds the first is refused outright now, so there is no path left to exercise; and the private runtime in a second game's prefix, the plugin process's own half of the Decky stop spin, and the bridge refused as disconnected are dropped as not worth the device sessions they would cost. The stop spin's established half stays where it was recorded, in section 2, along with the one thing about it nobody has watched directly. The names of the maintainer's own games and downloaded tables are out of the durable documents with them: what those rows were evidence for - six tables across three games and three sources, three of them written by one Cheat Engine release and three by another - does not need them, while a Valve title named for what Steam's own catalog says about it does.
- [Changed] `README.md` says what the panel actually does now. Its table of compatibility marks still described a grey question mark this version replaced with the second green check, and it presented `Local file` as a press on the table row when that row carries Search and Manage and nothing else. It also now says that opening a local file needs no game, what the start refusal about a target that is not running offers, and that the game list leaves out what Steam's own record says is not a game, including the one game that costs: one added from its own `.desktop` entry.
- [Fixed] A local file opened with no game chosen is reported by what actually happened to it. The import is durable the moment the backend answers, and the status read after it is reconciliation; awaited, one that failed for a moment reported the whole press as a failed import for a table that is on the device, and the reader's next move is to import it again. It is the same commit boundary Delete already draws.
- [Fixed] A support bundle no longer reports an item it could not collect when there was simply nothing to collect. A journal window that was read successfully and held none of this plugin's records is what a healthy device that has not run it lately looks like, and a panel journal that has not been flushed to disk yet is what a bundle taken shortly after the panel loads looks like. Both notes stay, because a reader looking for those lines should find out why they are not there.
- [Fixed] `target_plugin_install.py install --remote` quotes every value it sends and forwards every option it accepts. A plugin root holding a space broke in half and one holding a shell character was interpreted by the remote shell, while `--decky-url` and `--json` were accepted here and dropped on the way, so a custom endpoint was silently ignored and a caller parsing JSON got a summary line. In JSON mode what the far end wrote to stderr is relayed to stderr rather than folded into the report.
- [Fixed] Steam's own application cache is read past a UTF-16 value without losing its place. Two bytes per character and two zero bytes to end it means an ordinary Latin letter carries a zero byte of its own, so searching for the first zero pair finds one a byte early, leaves half a terminator in the stream and reads every field after it from the wrong place - including the whole launch block the read is for. Scanned a character at a time now.
- [Fixed] An installed app whose only Windows program is written for a named branch stays in the picker. The classification asks whether such a program exists on any branch, and asking for no branch in particular is not that - it means the branch nobody named, which keeps the public entries and drops the rest - so a user actually running a beta lost that game off the list.
- [Fixed] A game closed since it was proven is compared against its own program. One game can hold a record for two of them - a launcher and the game it starts - and the path the offline comparison read was the newest of those whatever program it was about, so a table proven on the game was checked against the launcher's version: a retest nobody owes, or, where one build stamps both files alike, a match nothing checked. The path is now looked up by the game and the program together.
- [Fixed] A pinned cheat that needs a value is not switched on without one. Configure refuses exactly that, but pinning commits on its own press, so closing the picker with Cancel never reached the refusal and left a switch on the panel - which has nowhere to type a number - that could only ever be pressed empty. The panel now refuses it and says where the value is entered. One value answers all three questions that row asks - what it displays, what has to exist before its switch may be pressed, and what that press writes - resolved as the number Cheat Engine holds, then the choice confirmed for this table, then the one Configure stored. Three separate resolutions meant a row could read `100` while the press sent nothing and enabled the cheat on whatever the game held, or read `40` while the press wrote `100`. Which of the three answers is chosen by whether it can be displayed; the answer itself is written exactly as it was stored, because trimming for a row and writing into a game are different jobs and doing both in one place rewrote ` 100 ` as `100`.
- [Fixed] A target this game is proven not to be running blocks the launch it cannot survive. The panel already said so in its own row and told the reader to repair it before starting, while the press underneath stayed live and Auto-load kept launching; the attach then waits for a process that is not there, which on this panel is indistinguishable from Cheat Engine failing. Manual Start re-reads what the game is running at the moment of the press, and Auto-load checks the same fresh observation it already takes. An observation that proves nothing still starts.
- [Fixed] The Approve screen lets the device's own proof outrank a name. A game whose files hold no Windows program at all had that fact hidden the moment the table named a process or Steam's record carried one, and `Use this table` stayed pressable over an `.exe` that does not exist here - which is the whole of what the warning is for. Only a Windows process actually seen running overturns the walk now, because that is the device contradicting itself rather than a file or an account describing a depot the other machine has.
- [Fixed] A game whose manifest or shortcut store could not be read stays in the picker instead of disappearing from it. A library folder this process may not list was answering `Path.glob` with no matches, which reads exactly like a library holding no games, and a shortcut store that was there and corrupt counted as a store read successfully; both are now reported as the incomplete reads they are, and the panel keeps every entry when it is told so. Only a read that failed does that: a Steam user folder holding no `shortcuts.vdf` is a user who has added nothing, which is an answer, and treating it as a failure put the whole account's shortcuts back on the picker of the one device most certain not to hold them - a clean one. A library holding more manifests than this reads is an incomplete read too, rather than a slice presented as the whole library. A single manifest whose bytes cannot be read still names the app it is about, so that app is counted as installed, which is what an unreadable install state already did.
- [Fixed] Upgrading from the previous version keeps what this device had proven and what it had marked as not working. Both records gained one optional field this version, and both readers accept exactly one shape, so the file each of them found was refused as corrupt: the not-working list read as empty, and with it the failure epochs every positive record is checked against, which turned every table the device had proven back into one needing a retest - and the first table proven after that rewrote the positive record with itself as its only row. Each reader now also reads the shape before its own, which is its own shape with that one field absent, states the field absent rather than guessing at it, and writes the file back in the current shape. Anything older is still a record the user clears.
- [Changed] A local file can be opened with no game chosen. The file joins this device's library, which is what the backend always allowed, and the association and authorization wait for the game the reader picks: Use on the row the import produces is where those happen. The whole section used to disappear instead, leaving no sign the route existed on the one screen that carries it.
- [Fixed] The weakest positive compatibility state is drawn as the success it is: the same green check as the strongest one, with a lightly dashed ring saying that nothing here compared the table with the build in front of the reader. It used to be a grey question mark, which on a list where an untried table carries no mark at all could only read as ignorance about a table the device had in fact proven.
- [Fixed] A table this device has proven now carries a mark on every screen that lists it. Search looked only for the selected game's own record, so a table another game here had proven showed exactly what a table nobody has tried shows: nothing. It falls back to that other record with the build claim dropped, which is the same rule Manage already used.
- [Changed] Manage's filter takes the width the line beside it is not using instead of stopping at a number chosen in the file, and that line is short enough to fit what is left, revealing itself while the reader is on the row. The list is headed by one row whether or not a game is chosen: with none, a bare sentence used to sit above that heading, a field carrying a description and no label, which Steam draws at description weight, so it read as an offcut with the real heading directly under it.
- [New] `target_plugin_install.py install --remote USER@HOST` installs on a device across the network in one call. It sends that exact artifact to the mirror of this checkout there rather than trusting whatever the mirror holds, forwards `--plugin-root` so a first install on a fresh device works the same way, and refuses outright when the mirror's copy of the helper is not the one being run - which is the staleness that arrangement invites and which used to present as a helper disagreeing with the source in front of you.
- [Changed] Reshoot the published screenshots and the README strip for this version. Every tile is a screen as it stands now rather than as it stood at 0.9.15: the panel with a live session and a cheat switched on, search showing a table already on this device instead of three failed downloads, review on a table whose source is the one the reader would recognise, cheat configuration with a row opened onto what `More` holds, and diagnostics cut above this device's own paths - the trim that does that is deeper now, because the rows above it are shorter.
- [Fixed] The Approve screen drew its process row as a block that stopped four fifths of the way across the window, with Refresh standing in the gap beside it: Steam's own field is what paints a row's ground, and that field was only as wide as the dropdown inside it. The row is one field now, holding the control and the press together, which is how every other row on that screen is built.
- [Changed] Manage draws the tables belonging to the game in front of the reader on a grey-blue ground. They used to be the plain rows in a list whose other rows were a step darker, so the list read as one run of grey and the rows that matter were the ones with nothing said about them. A hue rather than another step of grey, because a list read in one column reads the step and not which way it went, and no accent edge, because that edge is what marks the row heading a list.
- [Changed] Configure cheats gives the section picker and the filter a row each on a screen with the height for two. Halving a row halves the control, and Steam sets a dropdown's label beside its value, so on a 4K television the pair bought nothing and left `All supported controls` reading `All su...`; a handheld keeps them side by side, where the row they save is a cheat.
- [Changed] A handheld draws these screens in a smaller hand rather than with a row fewer: the heading, the two lines a row is set in, the gap between cheats, the padding Steam's own dropdowns and text boxes take, and the row a paged screen ends with are all tightened together, and Configure cheats says its counts on one line with the stored-only sentence behind the question mark that row already carries. On a Steam Deck that is five cheats a page where this version had been heading for three, and five tables in Manage; a television is untouched.
- [Fixed] The focus ring reaches Next again on Manage and on the section list in Look inside. Asking Steam to enter a row at its preferred child does nothing unless a child says it is that child, and splitting those two into separate props left every footer entering at Previous, which is disabled on the first page. The air under a stacked selector is back on a handheld too, on the game picker and the Approve screen: the short-screen rule that takes every field down to two pixels outranked the one that gives that selector its own. Manage's filter is wide enough to read a table name back in.
- [Fixed] A paged screen asks the box it is drawn in what that box is keeping out of sight, and gives up exactly those rows. The cheats window ended 15 pixels clear of Steam's bar by arithmetic and was scrolling by 10 anyway, so a screen that fitted still moved under the reader's thumb. `docs/DESIGN.md` states the whole rule and `docs/FIELD_NOTES.md` the measurements it rests on.
- [Changed] Delete plugin data keeps what a scope costs behind the row's own question mark, so the widest scope's list of directories fits a Steam Deck, and the Approve screen gives the process selector back the air under it that the dense rows had taken.
- [Fixed] The press that deletes plugin data turned its own label unreadable when the controller reached it: Steam shows a focused button by painting it light and its label dark, and this one's red tint was an inline style outranking that. The red is now a frame around the button, so Steam's focused appearance is untouched and the label is always readable.
- [Fixed] A paged screen checks, after it has sized itself, whether what it drew actually ends above Steam's bar, and gives up exactly the rows it did not. Manage measured what sat above its list before the filter had been drawn and ran 27 pixels behind that bar on a Steam Deck, which no arithmetic on the earlier measurement could have noticed.
- [Changed] Every paged screen lands the focus ring on Next when the reader comes down into its footer, instead of on a Previous that is disabled on the first page and reads as a dead row. Manage carries its filter in the row that heads its list, labelled inside the box and wide enough to read a table name back, which is a table back on the page; Configure cheats labels its filter the same way and keeps the block those two controls are in separate from the list they narrow; and the section list in Look inside holds its height across pages.
- [Fixed] The game picker offered Steamworks Common Redistributables, in whatever language the user's Steam is in, and a device's desktop applications beside its games: a browser, an emoji picker, a streaming client. Both are now left out on Steam's own record rather than by name - an app typed `Tool` that declares no Windows program, and a shortcut Steam made out of an installed application's own `.desktop` entry. An app Steam types `Game` is untouched whatever it declares.
- [Fixed] The panel reader reported a drawing panel as "CE Decky is not drawing anything right now": its page expression read a value declared below the loop that read it, so every page holding one of this plugin's surfaces threw. A page that throws is now named instead of counted as a page with nothing on it.
- [Changed] Configure cheats puts Section and Filter in one row, and keeps its tip only on a screen with room for it. Four cheats a page on a Steam Deck where there were two.
- [Fixed] A screen that fits itself to the display left 16 pixels under its footer where Steam's own modal puts 26, so a window could end behind the bar Steam paints along the bottom by less than one row. The padding is now the measured one, and each window is asked afterwards whether it cleared the bar: one that did not gives up exactly the rows it ended past it, whatever Steam did with its position.
- [Changed] Put the two presses on the delete screen in one controller group with the destructive one marked, take the way out of Plugin data on disk down to a small button, shorten the Approve screen's notes behind their own question mark, and page the code view's section list by what the screen fits instead of a press that adds twelve more rows.
- [Fixed] Keep a window above the bar Steam paints along the bottom of every Game Mode page. Measured at 41 pixels on both a Steam Deck and a 4K Steam Machine, it covers whatever is behind it, so a screen that fitted the page was still cut off; every screen that fits itself now reserves it.
- [New] Add `scripts/target_panel_read.py`, which reads what the plugin is showing as text: every row by its test id, the controls beside it with their state, and each height. A dozen lines where a screenshot is a megabyte, and it can open the quick access panel on this plugin's page through Steam's own navigation rather than by faking a press.
- [Fixed] Fit a page by measuring the row it is made of rather than by a constant written beside the stylesheet that draws it, so trimming a row for a short screen cannot leave the arithmetic dividing by the old height.
- [Changed] Give a short screen its space back: a text field's label sits beside the input rather than above it, and a row gives up two pixels at each end. On a Steam Deck a search screen spent 371 of its 534 pixels on chrome before the results started.
- [Fixed] Fit a page of tables in Manage to the screen it is drawn on. It was the one paged list here that measured nothing and took six as the answer for a handheld, and six ran off the bottom of a Steam Deck's page; the held window height now belongs to the page size it was measured for, so a screen that settles on three rows is not padded to the height of six.
- [Fixed] Size a search page against the screen the reader settles on rather than the one that exists while the search runs. The measurement was latched at the first layout, and this screen searches the moment it opens with its own status rows above the list: on a Steam Deck that was 371 pixels of chrome and two results a page, against 289 and four once the results were in, for the same game on the same screen. Only a smaller measurement may replace the held one, which is the direction the ratchet this latch exists for never reads.
- [Fixed] Stop a support bundle reporting nothing to collect as something it could not collect. A device that has never launched Cheat Engine has no launch logs and a plugin reloaded a moment ago has no log file yet; both were counted as failures, so a healthy Steam Deck said two items were missing. The notes stay in the archive, because a reader looking for those files should find out why they are not there.
- [Fixed] Explain a search that is merely slow rather than only one that is very slow. The panel waited ten seconds before saying which source it was still on, and three searches measured on a Steam Deck took 4.3, 6.7 and 12.4 seconds: two of them finished before the threshold and the third reached it with one poll to spare, so eleven of those twelve seconds were a spinner and a count.
- [Changed] Say that a game installed as a Linux build has nothing for Cheat Engine to attach to, instead of asking for an .exe basename that does not exist on this device. A Steam Deck's Half-Life 2 holds hl2.sh and hl2_linux and not one Windows program, while Steam's record names the one from the Windows depot another machine has. Only a completed walk of the game's own folder says this; a walk that hit its bound keeps the manual entry, because that says nobody looked rather than that there is nothing.
- [Fixed] Offer only the library entries this device actually holds. Steam's library belongs to an account, so a second device listed every title the first one has: a Steam Deck beside a Steam Machine offered 39 non-Steam shortcuts while its own store held 6, and eleven of its twenty-two installed apps were Proton builds and Steam Linux Runtimes, which are installed and are not games. What decides now is the device's own files: a manifest that says fully installed, a shortcut this device's own store holds whose program is on this disk, and whether Steam has anything at all to start for the app. Not what Steam calls the app: it types Half-Life 2's three episodes as tools, and those are games people cheat in. A library that cannot be read keeps every entry rather than hiding one.
- [Changed] Open 0.9.26.

## 0.9.25 — 2026-09-13

- [Changed] Let `qa.py --stage` name several stages instead of silently keeping the last of them, and print the exact install command for the package a run produced beside its digest.
- [Changed] Refuse a helper under `scripts/` that `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` has never heard of. The contract already said every helper is documented there and nothing checked it, so a helper could be added that the next agent would never find and would write a one-off command instead of. Four were exempt on the first run, each with the documented helper that runs it named as the reason.
- [New] Add `scripts/qa_durations.py`, which says which tests the gate's seconds are actually in. The answer was 91 of 108 seconds in 30 tests out of 906, and it is what any figure about the gate's cost should be taken with.
- [Changed] Take the release gate from 2m03s to 84 seconds by stopping its own tests waiting on things they are not testing: a fixture that rebuilt a four megabyte journal window by re-summing its list on every line, the recovered-supervision tests waiting out the real one second tick of the loop they drive, and the listing-crawl tests paying the real pause between pages while asserting which pages are read. Nothing the product does changed, and the tests that are about pacing keep theirs.
- [Fixed] Stop Manage moving a couple of pixels between its first page and its last. A page of six rows is six rows plus the five gaps between them, and the held height counted only the rows.
- [Fixed] Put air back between Apply and the way out in Configure cheats. The shared footer's right hand slot had no gap in it, which nothing noticed while every other screen puts one control there and which drew this screen's two as one wide button.
- [Fixed] Draw a paged screen's way out at the size of the paging controls beside it. Back and Close were a size up from Previous and Next on the same row, so one row of navigation read as two kinds of control; a window that ends in a decision rather than a list keeps the larger size.
- [Changed] Give Configure cheats and the code view the same footer as Search and Manage: paging in the middle, what the screen is for against the right edge, one row. The code view used to page from a row inside the script with the way out on a row below it, which is two rows of a window whose whole purpose is fitting more lines of code on screen.
- [Changed] Take the exact Windows program a Steam game starts from Steam's own record of it, rather than offering everything a walk of the game's folder found. Half-Life 2 is the case: its folder holds 28 executables, 27 of them SDK compilers under bin/, and Steam holds one line, hl2.exe, for the branch this device installed. The walk is what is left when that record says nothing or names a program this device does not hold, and the row now says which of the two a name came from.
- [Changed] Scroll the game's name and the table's name under the controller ring on the panel itself. Both are routinely longer than the 300 pixel column they share with two presses, and the compatibility mark that now leads the table's name takes more of that line again, so the end of each was the part a reader could not see.
- [Fixed] Hold Manage at the height a whole page of tables takes, measured off a row rather than computed, so a last page of two and a device whose whole library is three tables leave the window exactly where a full page left it.
- [Changed] End Manage with the row the search screen ends with: paging in the middle as two small controls, the way out beside them, and one row that is there whether or not the list has a second page. Paging used to be a row inside the list with a full width Back under it, so filtering a library down to one page moved every control on the screen.
- [Fixed] Put the panel's compatibility mark before the table's name rather than beside its buttons. That row carries a filename and two presses in a 300 pixel column, and a third thing in the trailing group laid Manage off the edge of the panel, which is the same arithmetic that kept the row to two controls in the first place.
- [Fixed] Reserve a fixed slot for a row's mark only on the screen that needs its buttons in one column. Reserving it everywhere took twenty-two pixels out of the quick access panel's one narrow row for a mark that is often not there, and moved Search and Manage for nothing.
- [Fixed] Hold a short last page at the height a full page measured, instead of multiplying a count of missing rows by a pixel constant that is a copy of what the stylesheet does and was already out by a couple of pixels in the cheat picker and visibly out in the results list.
- [Fixed] Put back the eight pixels between a row's two buttons. The gap moved into a custom property so one screen could tighten it, and its fallback was written as zero, which read as a deliberate rule and overrode the gap the action group sets: every pair of buttons on every screen ended up touching.
- [Fixed] Withhold Use in Manage from a table another game owns. A cheat table is written against one game's code, and the press did more than fail: it associated the table with this game before the review opened, and cancelling that review did not take it back, so one press put another game's table permanently into this game's library. A table no profile claims keeps the press, because it is the only route back after a recovery that discards the profile store.
- [Fixed] Hold a short last page open in Configure cheats and in search results, so reaching the end of a list no longer changes the height of the window and moves Apply and the way out of the screen with it.
- [Fixed] Keep the tighter row spacing to the one screen it was asked for. It was written as a rule every dense row obeyed, so it reached the quick access panel, whose rows carry the same controls in a far narrower column, and moved every button on it.
- [Fixed] Keep the tighter row spacing to the one screen it was asked for. It was written as a rule every dense row obeyed, so it reached the quick access panel, whose rows carry the same controls in a far narrower column, and moved every button on it.
- [Fixed] Stop offering to discard a cheat form that was put back the way it opened: the count was of records the reader had touched, so switching a cheat on and off again still asked what to do about one unapplied change.
- [Changed] Report a pinned cheat with no value when Apply is pressed rather than when it is pinned, and never disable either switch for want of one: a staged control that cannot be pressed is a screen refusing to be used and leaving the reader to work out why.
- [Changed] Give a row's controls less air and less space between them, and draw the search summary as the row that heads its results, so more of a name is readable and the counts are not read as one more result.
- [Fixed] Keep a row's controls in the same columns down the whole list. The mark now holds a slot whether or not there is a glyph in it, and every control on a table row is one width, so a mark appearing no longer pushed the buttons sideways and Use no longer moved depending on whether Revoke or Delete was beside it.
- [Fixed] Show the state of a table in Manage whichever of this device's games it belongs to. The evidence was filtered to the selected game, so every row in the other group showed exactly what a table nobody has tried shows; each of those rows names its own game, and the state was already computed against that game, so it is kept rather than downgraded.
- [Fixed] Keep the search summary to one line, revealed under the controller ring, so the panel stops jumping when Retry appears: that button shows up only when the page has marked rows, it takes width from the text beside it, and the per-source tally then reflowed onto another line and moved the whole list.
- [Changed] Put what a reader can do about a table's state on the row's own line in Manage rather than behind a help button, because the row already reveals its whole description under the controller ring.
- [Fixed] Keep a proven table proven once its game stops. The evidence now records the path its fingerprint was read from, so the same question can be asked off the disk later: a non-Steam shortcut has no Steam build id and its executable version is readable only while it runs, so a shortcut's green became current build unknown the moment the game closed, which is the state Manage is usually opened in.
- [Fixed] Show the state of the table a game is actually on, on the panel itself: it was the one table with no mark anywhere, saying marked as not working in words and nothing at all about a table proven to work, so the state of the table in use was the one a reader had to open another screen to see.
- [Changed] Check the plugin's own text for em dashes as well as the documents, including the escaped spelling and without treating a template literal as a quoted code span, because the one place the rule was not enforced is the text a user actually reads and a note in Configure cheats had one.
- [Changed] Say a refusal in Configure cheats once, beside the button that earned it, on its own colour and under its own heading, and drop it as soon as the reader does anything else: it was printed twice, read as one more row of the list, said Cannot apply for a refused pin, and could only be cleared by a successful Apply.
- [Changed] Put the whole of what Configure cheats does with nothing running on one line, with the rest behind its own question mark, because three sentences in a plain field wrapped to five lines above a list counted in rows.
- [Changed] Say what an enclosing script costs the reader instead of why Cheat Engine needs it. The note under a value in Configure cheats explained that the parent script is what creates the record's address, which is a sentence about internals in a place somebody came to set a number; it now says only that something else is about to be switched on, or that the value is stored and not live yet.
- [Fixed] Refuse to pin a cheat that has no value: the panel shows a pinned cheat as a switch with nowhere to enter one, so pinning one empty built a control whose only possible use was the thing Apply already refuses, reached by a route that never passes through Apply.
- [Fixed] Refuse to apply a cheat this press switches on while the value it needs is empty, naming which one, because Cheat Engine freezes whatever the game happens to hold at that address: it was accepted, written into the table's startup state and reported as applied, and the next session switched it on the same way.
- [New] Say on the panel, and offer the repair under Advanced, when a game is running and the process saved for it is not among the Windows programs it started, because that is the first real check of a target chosen from the game's files and pressing start with the wrong name attaches to nothing.
- [New] Offer the Windows executables in a game's own installed folder when a table names no process and the game is not running, which is the ordinary case and the one that could not be finished at all: a Steam library entry's launch executable exists only in the running game's process table, so Use this table stayed disabled until the game had been launched once.
- [Changed] Name the game beside each AppID under Debug, Sessions by app, put that list last on the screen and page it, because it is the only list there that grows on its own and everything a reader came for was behind it.
- [New] Fit a page of cheats and a page of search results to the screen they are drawn on, measured from the list's own top and the footer under it rather than from a figure estimated for chrome that is Steam's own controls and a banner that is there only sometimes.
- [Changed] Lead a search result with the date, then the release, then the size, which is what a reader scans a post's revisions by, and leave the glyphs and chips outside the moving line so they are readable the moment the row is.
- [Fixed] Keep one search result to one fixed-height row: its detail line asked for an ellipsis without asking to stay on one line, so a row carrying a file name, a source, a date and a size ran to four lines and a page of results was a different height on every search.
- [Changed] Show a cheat's whole name, its full group and what its author wrote about it when More opens it, and stop repeating a cut copy of the group on the row and again on the record line under it.
- [Fixed] Keep one cheat to one fixed-height row in Configure cheats, scrolling its name and its note under the controller ring the way Manage does, because a Cheat Engine record is named in the field its author also writes notes in and those names wrapped to five lines, so a page of six could not be read without scrolling the window.
- [New] Keep the release a source advertised beside a not-working record, because that record outlives the file it is about: one post carries every revision of a table under one filename, and months later the list had nothing to tell two of them apart. A record written before this reads the release from a copy still on the device.
- [Changed] Lead a row in Tables that did not work with the day, the release and the game build it was tried against, and shorten the recorded reason to its first sentence, because the sentence it used to open with is 230 characters and put all three past the end of the line. The whole reason stays on the row's own help and in the support archive.
- [Changed] Lead a row in Manage with what it is doing in a few words and then its release, instead of a sentence naming the screen the mark is cleared on: the release a reader is choosing between was past the end of the line in exactly the states where choosing wrong costs something.
- [Fixed] Stop showing a table's Cheat Engine format number as though it were the table's release: a row with no release from its source was labelled v46, which is the version of Cheat Engine's own table format and reads 45 or 46 for every table on a device whatever its game. A row now claims a release only where a source stated one.
- [Changed] Say once, in a support archive's own README, that it carries this plugin's journal records and never Decky's loader lifecycle lines, instead of noting it against the member on every bundle: the loader writes those as root and this backend can never read them, so the note was a finding no collection could avoid.
- [Fixed] Keep the system-journal records a support bundle was given instead of cutting them at half a megabyte, because the line cap above it is the deliberate bound and the byte cap was silently dropping several hundred records from the one log that survives a plugin reload, out of a whole-bundle budget with thirty-one of its thirty-two megabytes unspent.
- [Fixed] Stop reporting a support bundle as missing a file when every member is there: the panel counted anything the collector did not call truncated, and a third kind of note added later meant every healthy archive on every device said one item could not be collected.

## 0.9.24 — 2026-09-13

- [Security] Write out every journal line this plugin publishes in full, instead of accepting whatever followed a phrase Decky writes, because a component tag and an opening phrase are message text that another plugin can print, and its paths and game names rode the rest of the line into an archive attached to a public issue.
- [Fixed] Count the records Decky's own loader wrote from the journal file a support bundle actually carries, because that file is cut twice after the counting and a lifecycle line older than either cut was reported as present while the archive held none of it.
- [Fixed] Say in a support bundle when its system-journal window holds none of Decky's own records, because the plugin backend runs without the journal group and cannot read them, and four thousand of this plugin's own lines otherwise read as a complete journal with no restarts in it.
- [Fixed] Say in the panel's durable record how much was lost before each batch rather than how much the session has lost altogether, so one burst of dropped entries no longer marks every record written after it.
- [Security] Keep another plugin's lines out of the support archive: the journal filter accepted any line from Decky's own components that merely mentioned the webhelper, which could publish a foreign plugin's paths and game names in an archive attached to a public issue.
- [Changed] Space webhelper replacements on the same wall clock Decky counts them with, instead of a boot identity and a second clock that modelled a window Decky does not keep and charged an ordinary install a minute it did not owe.
- [Changed] Keep one implementation of the rule that a developer helper never guesses the Steam user home, instead of the same refusal copied into two provider surveys.
- [Changed] Report the Decky settings directory and Steam user home from the last install record when the live backend cannot be asked, checked against the filesystem again, because that is the one moment a probe needs them and they were reported as nothing.
- [Fixed] Place each panel row on its own side of a frontend reload by when its renderer started, because Decky does not reliably ask the frontend being replaced to import a newly installed plugin and it can therefore be identified by nothing else.
- [Fixed] Read the panel's record from where it actually ends when this plugin is being installed again after a removal, because an uninstall leaves that record in place and starting from nothing made its oldest rows look like the frontend this install is replacing.
- [Fixed] Pace the background listing crawl and its give-way after a search on a clock that cannot be corrected, so a system time change cannot hold the next pass for an hour and a half or let it start beside the search it should wait for.
- [Fixed] Wait for the frontend an install replaces to say which renderer it is, instead of assuming any renderer the record has not seen is the new one, so a row written on the way out can no longer be reported as the panel the install produced.
- [Fixed] Validate the panel record through the file that is actually open rather than through its name, so a trim landing between the two cannot have its retained tail read as newly appended evidence.
- [Fixed] Record which frontend generation wrote each panel row, so an install cannot read what the frontend it replaced wrote on its way out as the panel it produced.
- [Fixed] Measure every bounded wait in the panel on a clock that cannot be corrected, so a system time change cannot stretch a twelve second wait into minutes with every other press refused, or end it early and report work that was still running as gone.
- [Fixed] Read the panel's record from where the file ended rather than from a moment on its clock, so a clock corrected backwards cannot hand the previous frontend's rows to this install.
- [Changed] Say what the panel holds after an install in every case but the quiet one, so a panel with no CE Decky row, or one whose count cannot be reconstructed, is no longer indistinguishable from exactly one row.
- [Fixed] Give two panels of one loaded module a single hand-over queue for the record they share, because two queues over one cursor send the same entries twice and can leave the record ending before the entry that closed it.
- [Changed] Say in the operating contract that an install reports what the panel ended up holding rather than proving it, because the record it reads is allowed not to arrive, and refuse the old wording in the repository rules.
- [Fixed] Give each panel Decky adds an id of its own in the durable record, because two imports inside one millisecond share a loaded module and everything in it, so the record could show two rows as one and a dismount could take the other's evidence with it.
- [Fixed] Read what the panel ended up holding as the panels never dismounted rather than as a count of imports, so an ordinary re-import is not reported as a duplicated row and does not send anyone to replace Steam's webhelper for nothing.
- [Fixed] Take Decky's own install reply as proof that the plugin is not about to be loaded again, rather than four seconds of one process, because the process a stop is already killing is the only one running for longer than that.
- [Fixed] Arm the background pass in the state probe exactly when the plugin would, so a Search marker the backend refuses, such as one from a clock that moved forward, is reported as refused rather than as a crawl that is running.
- [Fixed] Say a self-test found a warning when one of its non-blocking checks failed, and name the check and its reason, instead of reporting an unqualified pass with the failure shown nowhere.
- [New] Count how many times the reloaded panel imported the plugin at the end of an install, because one row and two rows leave the same files, the same loader inventory and the same backend, and only the panel's own record tells them apart.
- [Changed] Let Decky finish replacing the plugin before asking it to reload the panel, because one install loads a plugin twice and a reload requested between the two loads left a second CE Decky in the panel over one backend.
- [Fixed] Wait out a fresh webhelper window when the clock has moved backwards, because an install that cannot measure how old the last replacement is may otherwise make the third one inside a minute, which is what stops Decky's service.
- [Fixed] Record a search in a small file of its own as it happens, so the background refresh it arms for a day survives a plugin update, a crash and a lost battery alike: the marker used to ride only in the listing cache, which is rewritten for it at most once an hour and which an unload cannot wait for while a crawl is writing it.
- [New] Have the self-test answer for the evidence a bug report depends on: the panel's durable record is writable and still private, this plugin's own system journal can actually be read, the managed root has room left, and the registered Cheat Engine is still the one that was registered.
- [Fixed] Collect the system journal into a support bundle again: journalctl inherited the loader bundle's library path and failed on every device, so the archive carried a note where the journal should have been.
- [Changed] Refuse the withdrawn lifecycle claim in the repository rules, because two static reviews read it, believed it and reported defects this host does not have.
- [Fixed] Keep the background listing timer alive after a tick that failed, so one full disk during a marker write cannot switch off the refresh for the rest of the session.
- [Fixed] Prove an owned self-test's prefix is clear after its process group is gone rather than before it, because a prefix asked about while Proton is still running answers for a moment that is already over.
- [Fixed] Take the newest of the two durable records of the last webhelper replacement, so a stale marker cannot declare Decky's window elapsed when a newer install says otherwise.
- [Fixed] Stop an owned Cheat Engine before writing anything at unload, so no write can spend the window the stop needs.
- [Changed] Bound the unload prologue as a whole rather than per owned launch, dropping only the expensive prefix pass when the budget is spent and always sending the signal.
- [Fixed] Leave Decky's own window between two plugin installs, because replacing Steam's webhelper three times inside a minute makes Decky stop its service and only root can start it again.
- [Changed] Report a self-test stop whose owned prefix could not be settled as exactly that, rather than borrowing the answer the empty process group gave.
- [Fixed] Keep one ordered writer for the provider index cache, so a native write that started earlier cannot replace the newer snapshot another one took, and believe only the marker the bytes that landed actually carry.
- [New] Call one method on the live backend from a tracked helper, target_plugin_rpc.py, for behaviour that only exists in the loaded plugin process.
- [Fixed] Stop the Cheat Engine a self-test started even when it has left the process group that started it, by retiring the owned prefix's Wine clients in the unload prologue as the orderly stop already did.
- [Fixed] Finish stopping an owned self-test Cheat Engine at unload: the group is signalled, given a bounded moment, killed if it stays, and confirmed gone, because the escalation that used to do this ran after an await that this host never reaches.
- [Fixed] Show a repaired game name on the read that found it even when the write that would keep it failed.
- [Fixed] Carry a cancellation that arrives while recovered supervision is draining, instead of reporting that unload released cleanly.
- [Fixed] Hold a focused row's overflow measurement while reduced motion is showing the whole of its value, so the wrapped line cannot withdraw the mark that wraps it and flip back and forth.
- [Fixed] Run the unload prologue before the diagnostics that suspend, so a loop already starved when the unload begins cannot take the work the prologue exists to guarantee.
- [Changed] Require one full release gate per batch of work in the operating contract, rather than one after every change.
- [New] Report what an install cost on its own summary line, so the bound a host needs comes from the helper rather than from reading the loader's journal by hand.
- [Fixed] Let reduced motion read the whole of a clipped row: the focused line wraps instead of scrolling, so a long filename and a recorded not-working reason stay reachable without animation.
- [Fixed] Repair every row of the blocked-table list in one write instead of one write per row, so opening Tables that did not work on a large part-named record is a read rather than hundreds of durable replacements of the same file.
- [Fixed] Stop an owned self-test Cheat Engine and write the owed search marker before the unload can suspend, because on this host nothing after an unload's first await is guaranteed to run.
- [New] Prove that the monitor for a Cheat Engine which outlived a plugin update keeps running after the load that resumed it returns, which is the property a review read this repository as denying.
- [Changed] Record what the plugin host actually does with event loops, one per process for its whole life, and stop a test name and a code comment from claiming otherwise.
- [Changed] Keep the install bound in the operating contract to the rule itself and move the measurement behind it to docs/FIELD_NOTES.md, where measurements belong.
- [Changed] Bound a plugin install in the operating contract with a timeout sized from what one costs on the host running it, because the helper reports its own failures in seconds and a bound of minutes only hides a hang.
- [Changed] Correct what was recorded about unload: the loop is not stopped for it, Decky's own stop closes the plugin socket and both ends of that socket then read at EOF in a loop that cannot suspend, which starves the loop the unload is waiting on.
- [Limitation] The plugin's event loop is not kept running for _unload() on this Decky build, so no await on the unload path completes and the backend is always killed five seconds after the stop request; what keeps plugin state safe is atomic replacement and recovery on the next load, recorded in docs/FIELD_NOTES.md.
- [New] Ask the event loop itself, at unload, whether it still runs a queued callback and whether it still fires a timer, because every drain below depends on both and their absence is the only evidence a stopped loop leaves.
- [New] Say whether a drain is waiting or spinning, because a cancellation redelivered on every resumption never reaches a report interval and leaves no record at all.
- [New] Record the event loop each lifecycle call runs on, and that unload's first suspension point came back, because a drain that never resumes is otherwise indistinguishable from one that was never reached.
- [New] Say what the backend is waiting on while it is still waiting, so an unload that outlives Decky's five second stop budget names the owner and the exact work holding it instead of ending the log where it stopped.
- [New] Record which owner the backend is releasing as it unloads, so a close that never returns names itself instead of ending the log where it stopped.
- [Fixed] Keep a search's arming of the background listing pass across a plugin reload, by writing the owed marker when the plugin unloads instead of leaving it to a scheduler tick three minutes away.
- [Fixed] Tell the panel a flush was kept only once every byte of it reached the file, so a write the device took part of cannot retire evidence from the panel's own copy as well.
- [Security] Keep the panel's durable record private to its user after the first size trim, which used to republish a file created 0600 with whatever the process umask gave it.
- [Fixed] Refuse a game change by reading the running state for that exact game at the press and again when a game is picked, so a game that started after the last poll or while the picker was open cannot have another game's context moved onto it.
- [Fixed] Keep the panel's durable record in the order it was recorded, by sending every hand-over, the last one at close included, one after another instead of letting the closing batch overtake the one still in flight.
- [Security] Keep another plugin's journal output out of the support bundle, by deciding a line is this plugin's from a shape only this plugin or Decky itself writes rather than from the product name appearing in it.
- [Fixed] Release the plugin cleanly when Decky unloads it, by never waiting at unload on background work that finished on the event loop the plugin loaded on.
- [Changed] Bound a QA run with a timeout sized from what a full run costs on the host it is running on, so a hang in the change being validated ends as a failed run instead of a foreground wait that cannot diagnose it.
- [Changed] Say in the operating contract that a reported problem is captured whole into build/incidents/ before anything is recovered, since recovery is what destroys that evidence.
- [New] Reach the panel's durable record and the journal from target_plugin_log.py, with --frontend and --journal.
- [New] Read what Steam's webhelper threads are doing when CEF will not answer the debugger at all, with target_webhelper_threads.py.
- [New] Collect this plugin's own records from the system journal too, which keeps them when the log file holding a failure is deleted by the next install.
- [New] Keep what the panel did where a restart of Steam's webhelper cannot delete it, so a panel that froze can still be read afterwards.

## 0.9.23 — 2026-09-13

- [Changed] Grey out Change while this profile's game is running without a row explaining it; README says why under Pick the game.
- [Fixed] Say why a game change is refused on a row that wraps, at the end of its section, instead of taking the game row's own line for it.
- [Changed] Refuse a game change while the game this profile is on is running, and say on the row that stopping it releases the press.
- [Changed] Refuse a game change while the game this profile is on is running, and say on the row that stopping it releases the press.
- [Fixed] Give the game picker's dropdown air under it, instead of sitting on the bottom edge of its own block.
- [Changed] Say a table is in use once on its row instead of twice, and slow the paced reveal in both windows by another fifth.
- [Fixed] Measure a row's line against the window it is shown through, so the reveal no longer cancels itself the moment the row is focused.
- [Changed] Read a wider front of the listing on the first search after a long gap, where the newest topics are.
- [Changed] Arm the FearLess listing refresh with Search: one search brings the index up to date and the crawl then goes quiet, so a device nobody searches on asks the forum for nothing.
- [Changed] Pace the reveal in Manage and in Tables that did not work by the length of the line, so a long sentence no longer moves past the reader at twice a readable speed.
- [Fixed] Start a row's reveal whichever way Steam marks the focus on it, and measure the line again when the row is focused rather than only when it was built.
- [Changed] Run the repository rules first and stop the run when one fails, and refuse a comment written between an element's props, which is what made the tracked bundle carry trailing whitespace.
- [Fixed] Reveal both lines of a table's row in Manage and in Tables that did not work by scrolling them while the row has focus, the way a pinned cheat's lines already do.
- [Changed] Keep the heading of Tables that did not work to what cannot be read off the entries under it, and move the rest behind its own press.
- [Fixed] Fill in the game on a not-working record that was written before that game had a profile, instead of leaving the row naming a file and no game.
- [Fixed] Lead each row in Manage with the game whose library holds the table, so a file name no longer has to name a game on its own.
- [Fixed] Wait out a Cheat Engine that has gone quiet running a table's own script rather than reporting it as disconnected, and say which of the four things a disconnected bridge actually is.
- [Changed] Say how many cheats a table declares beside how few of them carry code, so Look inside reads as a short list rather than a missing one.
- [Fixed] Hold each table's status glyph in Manage against the row's own controls, so a long table name can no longer hide it.
- [Fixed] Refresh the FearLess listing on a timer while the plugin is loaded, instead of only for as long as somebody is searching.
- [Fixed] Keep Cheat Engine's own "cannot read this yet" placeholder out of a cheat's value field, so an editor with nothing stored opens empty and a saved value is what stands in for it.

## 0.9.22 — 2026-09-12

- [Changed] Name the same four things the not-working record holds before anything is in it, including a download whose archive nothing here can open.
- [Fixed] Answer a status read that another read overtook and then failed with the winning read, so a press cannot be refused over an error about a question already answered.
- [Changed] Say in Advanced what the not-working record actually holds, including a provider row whose file a source no longer has, and that a download or source condition never refuses a copy already on this device.
- [Fixed] Make a status or not-working read that another read overtook wait for that read and answer with it, rather than with the snapshot from before it.
- [Changed] Say what marking a table as not working actually does: the file stays and search still shows it, nothing may newly use those bytes until the mark is cleared, and clearing removes that refusal alone.
- [Fixed] Report one whole current record when a not-working entry's provider rows are repaired, never yesterday's reason beside today's rows.
- [Fixed] Hand every caller the refresh that was accepted rather than one already overtaken, so a Manage confirmation and a detached Search cannot act on or repaint from a read this panel had rejected.
- [Fixed] Show a table whose success a later failure superseded as needing a retest in Manage with no game selected, the same as Search says for those exact bytes.
- [Fixed] Keep the saved-or-download window open when its Retry arrives while another press is still running, instead of closing without doing anything.
- [Fixed] Report what a not-working record actually holds after a repair whose durability could not be proven, instead of the wider set the repair had recovered.
- [Fixed] Report the provider rows a repaired not-working record actually kept, so the first read after a repair describes the same set as every read after it.
- [Fixed] Repair a record's provider rows from the newest the table carries, and take a failed table's proven mark away even when the list of tables that did not work is full, telling you what makes room.
- [Fixed] Keep a table you marked as not working marked when the record list fills up, making room from download and source failures instead, and record the provider rows those bytes were most recently found through.
- [Fixed] Keep a table recorded as not working marked until the user clears it, whatever a later download says about the same bytes, and date the evidence a clear releases so an older success cannot return green on its own.
- [Fixed] Keep a payload or source record from dating compatibility evidence or blocking a fresh proof in the backend, and from taking a proven table's green when Search clears it.
- [Fixed] Treat a record about a download or a source as exactly that everywhere: it no longer retires a search row holding a usable stored copy, no longer turns proven history amber after a re-read, and no longer reads as this table not working on Home or in Manage.
- [Fixed] Keep a verified stored table usable and its proven history visible in Search when a record about a download or a source names the same digest; only a record about whether the table works refuses that copy.
- [Fixed] Let a Retry clear only the records the row it is on is showing, so a stored copy marked as not working can be retried without discarding what is known about the file its source is offering now.
- [Fixed] Clear only the exact table a Retry is beside, keep an unreadable not-working list from refusing any new selection, and stop the saved-or-download window offering a copy it has already disabled.
- [Fixed] Refuse a new selection or authorization of a table marked as not working from every route, including Manage, and offer the press that clears the mark in Search for a stored table and in the saved-or-download window for a refused download.
- [Fixed] Wait for the panel transitions the managed-setup cancellation and the Manage filter actually schedule, so a loaded machine cannot fail the frontend gate on timing alone.
- [Fixed] Keep one exact table behind each compatibility mark, so a stored copy's record can no longer colour the newer revision a source is offering, and say plainly that Local means the bytes are present while using a copy marked as not working stays refused until it is cleared.
- [Fixed] Keep Local on a search result whose stored bytes are marked as not working, and mark both halves of a known duplicate pair even when one source publishes no checksum.
