Only a section's own name being longer than the line is cut now, at its end, so a name can never wrap and push the rest of the list down the screen. Measured by laying out all eighty section options of the ten tables on the development device: none is cut, where eight were, and none reads the same as another.
- [Changed] Say what happened to a retired search row, on the row's own title line. Every marked row said the same "Marked unusable", which repeated a word for a state the greying already shows and told a user nothing about what to do next, and it sat on a line of its own, which made a marked row taller than the rows around it. The record now carries which of three things put the entry there, recorded by the route that writes it rather than guessed at afterwards from a sentence: **Failed** is a table Cheat Engine ran a cheat from and it came straight back off, which a game update can make untrue; **Not a table** is a download that was never one, which nothing makes untrue; and **Gone** is the source saying it no longer has the file, which is not about this device at all. Each is a chip at the right of the title, as **Local** now is, and it takes no width from the panel: the title gives way to an ellipsis first.
- [Changed] Name a table that did not work by its game. **Advanced -> Tables that did not work** spans every game and is read months later, and only a table Cheat Engine had refused recorded which game that was: the other two routes wrote a bare archive name and a raw provider key, so the rows a search had just retired could not be found in the list they were in. The game now travels with the press that records the entry, for a download, a local import and a table that cannot be read alike, and the row is headed by the game and then the table. A row whose file the source has lost also keeps that file's own name instead of the key it is stored under. A game that cannot be read leaves an entry with no game rather than no entry.
- [Changed] The not-working record has one on-disk shape. Nothing is released, so a reader that carries every shape the file has passed through is compatibility code with no user behind it: a file older than the current shape reads as unreadable, which refuses no import and is repaired by the same **Clear all** as any other unreadable record.
- [Fixed] Four defects found reviewing the whole of this version. A table nesting three thousand elements parsed and then raised `RecursionError` out of the new code view, which is not the failure every caller of it is written to expect: only Cheat Engine's own entry nesting was bounded, and general nesting now is. A window the table carries was reported twice, once as the container holding it and once as itself, and the container half was empty because a window's definition is written below the element that names it rather than in it; the same read made an embedded payload measure zero bytes. A line that lost an invisible character was reported as a section that had been cut short, because one counter stood for both, so the screen said both things whenever either was true. And the cheat picker dropped the group a lone section sits under: with one section nothing is being repeated, so nothing should be taken off it.
- [Fixed] Read a long script a page at a time. The page was eighty lines, which is taller than the window showing it, and a controller cannot scroll a block of text: Steam moves a panel by moving focus, there is nothing to focus inside code, so focus jumped from the row above it straight to Next and everything between the two was unreachable. The window is fixed now and the page is built to fill it once, counted in the rows a line takes rather than in lines, because a long line wraps into several of them. The block keeps that height on a last page holding two lines, so Previous, Next and Back do not move between presses.
- [Changed] Stop the cheat picker repeating what every section has in common. A table author's own structure routinely begins with instructions rather than with a category: one table nests every group under "[STEP 1] Auto Attach Process ..." and "[STEP 2] Enable / Initialize Table ...", so all nine options opened with the same 128 characters, each ran to three lines and the nine filled the screen. The groups they all share are dropped, and what is still too long gives up its front rather than its end, because the last group is the one that says which section this is.
- [Changed] Show the whole installer digest to whoever asks the row for it, and drop a row that said nothing. The artifact row shows twelve characters, which is an identity to recognise rather than one to check, and asking it what it meant gave a paragraph that did not contain the digest at all, so the full one was on no screen anywhere. "What vouched for it" was a row of its own restating why anything was installed: the digest being the reviewed one is the whole reason, so "matches the reviewed release" was the artifact row said again as a verdict. What it actually carried, the reviewed publisher and the pinned-key signature check, now belongs to the artifact it is about.
- [New] Look inside a table without leaving Game Mode. The screen that asks whether an exact SHA may execute what it carries offered a count of markers as the whole of the answer, and reading the scripts themselves meant Desktop Mode, a file manager and a text editor, none of which a user with a controller has: the question was being asked with no way to answer it. **Look inside this table** on that screen, and the same view beside the file under **Advanced -> Active table**, list what the table can execute and open any of it as text: its own Lua, each record's Auto Assembler script under the name of the cheat it belongs to, a window it brought with it, and the fact of an embedded file. It reads and runs nothing. Sections are listed without their text and fetched one at a time, because one real table here holds 529 KiB of scripts across 99 records; each is bounded and normalized exactly as a control label is, so a script cannot render as something other than what it is, and anything cut or removed is said on screen rather than silently applied. An embedded file is named and sized and its bytes never reach the panel, because those bytes are the one thing in a table that exists to be executed somewhere else.
- [Fixed] Offer a `.rar` table on every source that publishes one. What a row may carry and what each source's own listing is filtered by were separate copies of the same set, and only the first gained the format: FearLess, Playground, GitHub and The Cheat Script went on dropping those rows at their own parser, so the format this version added was downloadable from exactly one source out of five. There is one set now, read from where a provider row is validated, and a regression holds every adapter to it.
- [Fixed] Say that an encrypted `.rar` can never be opened, instead of asking for its password. The refusal is recognised by the sentence the archive layer writes, and that sentence names the format, so adding `.rar` left the check matching `.7z` alone: an encrypted member of a `.rar` fell through to the branch that asks for a password, which is the exact dead end the check exists to prevent. Both formats are now recognised from the one place the sentence is composed.
- [Fixed] Show one dialog for one failure. A press from the search screen crosses two reporters, the panel's own and the screen's, so a failed import or a failed Clear opened two windows that had to be dismissed in turn. The failure itself is marked as shown, which is exact where counting open windows would not be: two different failures at the same moment still each get one.
- [Fixed] Let the row that heads a list be reached and opened. It is that list's own first row of data rather than the caption above it, but it was left out of the rule that makes a read-only row a focus target, so Home's runtime line was the one row on the panel that could neither be scrolled to nor expanded to read the end of.
- [Changed] One mark on a retired search row, drawn as a mark. "Local" and the two retirement marks decide whether a row can be pressed at all, and they were the first word of a run-on line of file name, source, date and size in the same dimmed grey as the rest of it; each is now a filled chip, in the product's own accent for a table already on the device and in the colour of a refusal for one that will not be offered. Both marks were also landing on the row together, because the durable record and the mark that retires the row for this session are written from the same outcome, so it read "Marked unusable · Damaged table". The record is what is shown, and it is also the only one of the two that is right about a file the source no longer has.
- [Fixed] Record a table that cannot be read, so it stops being offered and can be cleared. An inspection refusal is a property of those exact bytes and of nothing else, and it was the one way a table could be unusable without being written down anywhere: it imported, joined the game's library, was offered as a local table in every later search, and every press ended in the same refusal with nothing to clear and no way back. It joins the list every other unusable table is in, so search greys the row with the reason, re-import is refused with it, and one press undoes all of it. A stored file that is missing or is not the bytes its digest names is deliberately not recorded, because that is a state of the device rather than anything about a table.
- [Fixed] Read a table whose item list is larger than a parser limit. One record's list of 6508 items was 12 KiB over the ceiling on how big such a list may be, and that refused the whole 323-entry table rather than that one list, which is what every other limit in the same parser already does. It drops the list and counts it now. Both ceilings are also measured against the tables that exist rather than picked: at 4096 values each of that table's three item pickers was silently losing 2412 of the items it exists to choose between.
- [Fixed] Keep an automatic retry out of the user's way. Auto-load is the one path with no press behind it, and routing every failed action to the new window put a dialog over a running game titled as something the user had just done. It reports where it did before.
- [New] A file a source no longer has is recorded where the user can see and clear it. It has no content to key on, so the record is keyed by the provider row instead and joins the same list a table that did not work is in, because to the user it is the same statement about the same row. Clearing it is the same press.
- [Fixed] Show a failed press in a window instead of a Steam notification. A notification slides away on its own timer and was gone before it had been read, and the panel row that also holds the message is usually behind whatever the workflow opened next. The window states what failed and keeps the backend's own detail on a second row, for a bug report. A notification is still the fallback for a window Steam refuses to open.
- [Fixed] Say when a source is busy rather than preparing. One source serves a guest a single download at a time and answers a second with its own refusal, which is waited out. Reported as the ordinary countdown it read as the countdown starting again from the beginning for no reason anyone could see.
- [Fixed] Write back the provider rows a blocked record had to recover. An entry written before those rows were tracked knows only the digest, and the list filled that in from the table's own copy while leaving the record empty, so recognition depended on that copy still existing: lose it and a table the user had already proved broken was offered and downloadable again with nothing left to recognise it by.
- [Fixed] Stop offering a download that opened correctly and holds no table. An archive with no `.CT` inside is a property of those exact bytes and not of this device: the transfer worked, the archive read, and there is simply nothing in it, which is the same terminal answer a damaged table gives. The row is retired with it, and it is described as what it is rather than as a damaged file, which had been sending the user to look for a broken upload. Every other archive failure is left alone, because a 7-Zip without a handler or a member that could not be read are conditions that can change.
- [Fixed] Move focus onto the action that matters when a download ends. Steam repaints the focus ring where input moves it, and a row focused while the download ran changes underneath it when it stops, so the ring stayed on what was no longer there until the user pushed the stick, which reads as a dialog that broke rather than one that finished.
- [New] A file a source no longer has is said so and stopped being offered. It is one outcome for every source rather than one provider's quirk: a source that serves over plain HTTPS says it with 404 or 410, and one whose own client contract answers in its own vocabulary raises it from the adapter that understands that vocabulary. The row is retired for the rest of the session, because asking again reads the same however often it is done and on a source that makes a guest sit through a countdown first it costs that countdown to be told so. It is deliberately not added to the durable list of tables that did not work: that list is keyed by content and there is no content here, and a missing file is a fact about the page now rather than about bytes forever, so a re-upload is found again. The user is told which source answered, in a sentence; the source's own code and wording go to the log and the diagnostics, where the failed download is counted against it.
- [Changed] The spinner moved from the dialog's heading to under the countdown, in the block that names the file both belong to. It still takes no row of its own, so nothing below it moves down for the length of the download and back up after, and the right of that block is left empty once there is neither a countdown nor a transfer to report.
- [Changed] Plugin data on disk is headed by its own first row of data, like the two screens beside it: the verdict, what removal would cover and anything blocking it, with Delete on the row. It was a hand-built block with its own four styles, which is why it was the one summary that did not match.
- [Changed] Put a download's countdown on the right of the block that names the file. It was the tail of a sentence that never changes, and it is the only part of that row that changes every second, so reading it meant finding it again each time.
- [Changed] A list whose first row is its own summary is headed by that row: the totals for every table source, the count of tables that did not work, and Home's live runtime state are set a step darker than the panel and hold the rows that follow off themselves. The caption above them stays what it was, text and a rule. Both the tone and the gap are among the variables the panel declares, so either can be changed in one line. Advanced's stored-copy row also stopped telling the reader to expand it to see the whole path, which is something they had already done: that sentence is only readable in the expanded state it was describing. `.rar` reached the local file picker and the places that list what can be opened, which still named the three formats that came before it.
- [Fixed] Let a screen be scrolled to its end. Steam scrolls a panel by moving controller focus into it, so a run of rows with nothing to press on them cannot be reached at all: Debug, which is almost entirely such rows, stopped at whatever fitted on one screen and the rest was unreachable. A row with no control of its own, including one whose only action is disabled, is now a focus target itself, and pressing a truncated one wraps its text rather than doing nothing, which is what the `?` button does for a row that has one. Being a container is not enough on its own: one holding a control is a target because of that control, and one holding only text was observed to be reached only when it could be activated, so every such row is given an activation whether or not it has anything to open.
- [Changed] One section heading on every screen. Half the screens used Steam's own section title and half used CE Decky's heading with the rule beside it, so the same product looked like two depending on which modal was open. They all use the heading now, the download window keeps its spinner on that row, and everything the heading is made of, plus the accent beside a status row, is declared once as variables on the panel so the look can be changed in one place. Repository QA rejects the Steam title. Two Advanced screens also carried a tinted accent row directly under their heading, which read as a second header in a third style; those are ordinary rows of the list they introduce. The accent is left where it says something that changes while the user watches, above the cheats it describes.
- [Fixed] Stop handing Decky's own library path to the programs this plugin runs. The loader is a bundled interpreter that points `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` at its own libraries, every plugin backend inherits it, and each system binary inherited it again. On this device 7-Zip is a shell wrapper, so the shell resolved the bundle's readline and exited on an undefined symbol before 7-Zip ran: every `.7z` and `.rar` the plugin was asked to open failed that way, and the check that asks 7-Zip which formats it carries read the failure as a build with no RAR handler, which is how a device that can open RAR was dropping every `.rar` row from its searches. Cheat Engine, wineserver and the signature check were being started the same way.
- [New] Tables published as `.rar` can be downloaded. The sources publish in it and the row was being dropped before the user ever saw it: one game's only table at one source went that way, and on another search five of that source's six rows did. It opens through the same 7-Zip contract as `.7z`, with the same listing, the same single-member transfer and the same refusal of an encrypted member. Only the full 7-Zip build carries the RAR handler, so the resolved binary is asked once which formats it has and a device without it drops those rows at the search rather than after a countdown and a download.
- [Fixed] Find the tables a source has sorted to the end of its own listing. FearLess marks such a topic with a leading lowercase `z`, and scoring that as part of the game's name put the topic far below the floor a topic must clear to be fetched at all: searching for one game returned nothing from the source that had its table, downloaded thousands of times. On one device's index 132 topics carried the marker, current releases among them. Only the lowercase marker is one, because `Z Remastered` is a real game on the same listing.
- [Fixed] Stop one unnameable row from costing a whole source. A title that normalizes to nothing, which one live forum topic does, raised out of the ranking and took every result that source would have returned with it. It is a non-match now, like any other candidate with no identity to compare.
- [Fixed] Say a table is already on the device when it is. The mark that skips the download asked whether the selected game had imported the table, when what it answers is whether the bytes have to be fetched again, and content identity settles that on its own. The same table published for a second game, the same game reached through another shortcut, and a first import whose profile step failed after the bytes were stored all sat through another provider countdown for a file already on disk.
- [Fixed] Let a game accept its first table. The profile a game with no table yet has to be given is written and then read back, and the read reused the status the panel already had, which was taken before that profile existed: the import ended in "could not create the game profile" for a profile that was on disk, right after a toast saying the table had been imported. Nothing was added to the game's library and nothing was offered for review, so the only route back to bytes that were already downloaded, verified and stored was to fetch them again. A game that already had a profile skipped that step, which is why every later import worked.
- [Fixed] Give a VGTimes download the same window Playground gets. A source whose own chain makes the user wait before any byte moves opens a modal of its own, so the countdown, its Cancel and any archive choice after it are in front of the user instead of in a row at the bottom of a result list. The screen asked for one source by name, and VGTimes has exactly that chain twice over, a countdown its own client contract states and one download per guest at a time, so its wait ran in the list. Which sources those are is now declared in the provider registry beside the rest of a source's identity and carried on the download itself, so the next source with that chain cannot be added without it.
- [Fixed] Put the newest table at the top of a search. Results are ordered by how plausibly they match the game and then by date, and the relevance half was split on an even scale fine enough to turn noise into rank: the score is not a pure title measurement, since the source's own trust shifts it by hundredths, so two sources that recognised the same game equally plainly landed in different bands and a table five years old was offered above every table from this year. The bands are now the three the identity matcher already means by those numbers, confident, plausible and weaker than that, and inside one of them the date decides.
- [Fixed] Say that a download is waiting for its provider where the user can see it. That line lives at the bottom of the panel with the archive choice and Cancel, which is several screens below the press when the row was near the top of the results, so a provider countdown of a minute or more looked like a panel that had stopped responding. The same sentence is now shown above the results too, for exactly as long as the download is running.
- [Fixed] Name the game on a VGTimes row. The site files each entry inside the game's own section, so its title routinely names no game at all: "a table for Cheat Engine" is a complete entry name there, and in a list that merges five sources it reads as a table for something else entirely. The row now carries the game from the page's own breadcrumb, which is where the site declares it.
- [Fixed] Remove invalid controller-flow values from the panel path that wedged Steam's whole UI. Four horizontal groups described their layout as `right`, which the current Steam focus engine does not accept; while this panel was collapsing, both QAM and the Steam button stopped responding and that refusal was the only console diagnostic. Directional groups now use Steam's `row` contract through one component that counts enabled controls, so a disabled sibling cannot turn the wrapper back into the known one-button focus trap. Repository QA rejects both unsupported literals and groups that bypass that component.

## 0.9.13 — 2026-09-04

- [Fixed] Let searches resume when replacing provider counters is cancelled while it is still draining searches. Suspension marks searches unavailable before that drain waits, so cancellation at the wait used to skip the matching resume and leave every later search refused until the plugin was reloaded.
- [New] Table sources can be switched off. Every source is on to begin with, and Advanced now lists them with what each one has actually done on this device: how many searches it answered, how many tables it produced, how many downloads it finished or failed, how many of its pages could not be read, and whether it is currently waiting out a limit it asked for. A source is switched off from that screen, and the choice is durable and reversible. It is a preference and not a trust decision: nothing already downloaded from a source is affected by switching it off.
- [New] Switching a source off stops every route into it, not only its own search. A page one source publishes can name the exact repository or topic a table lives in at another, so a user who turned GitHub off still had GitHub contacted, and still saw a GitHub row, because a Playground page had pointed at it. That read is now refused with the rest, and the refusal is logged against the source that would have answered.
- [New] A switched-off source stops answering out of the search cache. Its rows are dropped where every re-served row passes through, which covers both ways a cached row reaches an answer: the stale fallback for a source that failed, and the whole cached answer used when a search returns nothing. A row already on screen when the source is switched off can no longer start a download either.
- [Changed] Search says which sources it did not ask. A source that is off is listed as off in the per-source line rather than dropped from it, because a deliberately narrowed search and a build that never had those sources produce the same empty answer otherwise, and a search that asked nothing at all now says so in a sentence instead of reporting no tables found.
- [Fixed] Stop reopening Search on an answer from a set of sources that has changed. Its cache is keyed by game and query alone, which was the whole of search identity until a source could be switched off: reopening then restored rows from a source that is no longer asked, still looking downloadable and only refused on the press, while switching one back on kept the summary saying it was off. Changing the choice now forgets every stored outcome, and an answer that lands after the change is not stored as current.
- [Fixed] Count rows against the source that served them. A page one source publishes can name the exact page a table lives on at another, and the whole batch was credited to the source that linked it: one Playground row and two GitHub rows read as three Playground results and nothing at all from GitHub, on the one screen that exists to say what each source is doing and offers to switch it off. Unreadable pages met on a linked page were misattributed the same way, and a linked read is now counted as itself rather than as a search of a provider nothing searched.
- [Fixed] Stop an ordinary search from rebuilding the provider cache a deletion has just removed. Closing the Search screen does not stop the search behind it, and only downloads and the background listing crawl were held down, so a search still running could write the results file, the provider counters and its own snapshot back afterwards: "rebuilt on the next search" became rebuilt from the previous one. Searches in flight are now drained and new ones refused for the length of the deletion.
- [Fixed] Stop a cancelled deletion from leaving downloads and searches refused. Quiescing them is two waits of its own, and cancellation at either one skipped the step that lets them run again, so a deletion that never happened could block every later download and search until the plugin was reloaded.
- [Fixed] Stop a report that could not be produced from reporting a deletion as failed. The files go before the panel is told what is left, and a failure reading that afterwards rejected the whole call: the user was told nothing had been removed for state that was already gone, and the panel skipped the catching-up it owes. The report is best effort now, and the panel reconciles whether the call succeeded or not.
- [Fixed] Drain searches before replacing the record of what each source has done. A search that started first is a writer of that record, so it put its own counters back into the one that had just replaced it, and the repair is now reconciled against the record the same way a source switch is.
- [Fixed] Stop describing a source by an attempt a later one superseded. A successful download left the HTTP status of the failure before it in place. The deadline a provider asked for is deliberately kept: a download somebody is watching is throttled by its own transfer rather than by the crawl's deadline, so succeeding during one is expected rather than evidence it is over, and the row now says so instead of reading as a contradiction.
- [Fixed] Reconcile a source switch whose reply was lost. It went straight through the ordinary action path, so a transport failure, or one raised after the file had already been replaced, reported that the switch failed for a choice that is stored. It re-reads the record and settles it, and no longer syncs the directory a second time after the shared atomic write has already done so and reported the outcome. Settling it asks whether the record holds the position the switch was for; the first attempt required every source in the list to hold it, which is false for every source other than the one being switched, so the path that exists to recognise a committed write recognised none of them.
- [Fixed] Offer only the repair that works while the source choice cannot be read. Each individual switch writes by reading that same record first, so every one of them was guaranteed to fail, and its generic error replaced the explanation of the corruption. They are disabled until the record is replaced.
- [Fixed] Stop showing invented zeroes for counters that could not be read. A source with nothing recorded and a whole counter record that is unreadable both produced a row of zeroes, in Advanced and in the support bundle summary, which states as fact that nothing has happened. The screen also claimed searching would rebuild that record: it cannot, because every recording path loads the whole file first and every caller ignores a failed load so a broken counter can never fail a search, so Advanced now offers to replace it.
- [Fixed] Say that deleting a game's saved setup also erases every table marked as not working. That record lives with the profiles, so the scope always removed it; the confirmation named everything else. The panel's own retirement marks and cached searches are dropped with it, and Advanced re-reads both lists rather than going on showing what the deleted files held.
- [Fixed] Clear a failed last attempt when the next one succeeds. A failed download writes that state, and a later successful one cleared only the error, so a source could report a finished download, no error and a failed last attempt at once.
- [Fixed] Follow a link under the selection the search began with. One search reads the choice once so its jobs, its cached rows and its summary describe one set of sources, and the linked read alone consulted the live value, so a switch pressed mid-search changed what that search read.
- [Fixed] Say which sources another source's page can actually name. Being searched was taken to mean being linked to as well, so all five were described as reached both ways when only two of them are. Which ones is now declared in the provider registry, and a linked read of a provider that does not declare it is refused.
- [Changed] The support bundle carries the source choice. The archive holds the record itself, the merged view of which sources are on beside what each one did, and a plain-text section in the summary, because a report that a game finds no tables is unanswerable without it: a source that was never asked and a source that answered with nothing look identical in every other file. Debug and the diagnostics snapshot state it for the same reason.

## 0.9.12 — 2026-09-04

- [Fixed] Stop turning a provider cooldown into a successful search. FearLess answers from its own cached forum index, so a cooldown made it return nothing rather than refuse, and every returned list was read as success: that cleared the deadline the provider had just asked for, told the user the source was fine with no results, and skipped the cached rows it would otherwise have fallen back to. A source can now report what it obtained and that it stopped, at the same time.
- [Fixed] Treat a Playground rate limit as one on every route it reads. A limit on the sitemap fell through to the cache and the crawl carried on asking the provider it had just been told to wait for, and a limit on a page or a category was indistinguishable from any other non-answer, so no cooldown was set and the diagnostics never learned it had been throttled.
- [Fixed] Give each source a bounded share of the search. Every request was bounded on its own, which is not the same thing: a provider whose whole chain times out request by request kept a search somebody is watching waiting for minutes while every other source had long finished. A source that has not answered within its budget is unavailable for that search, and the rest of the answer is delivered.
- [Fixed] Stop a challenged provider at every read it owns, including the ones the earlier fix missed. A challenge answered by a category listing degraded to the ranked fallback and the provider went on requesting item pages; a challenge answered while crawling a sitemap was read as one unreadable page, so the crawl asked the same challenged site for every remaining page and then went on to fetch the posts it had found. Each now stops that provider for the query, and a challenge part way through keeps the pages that did answer.
- [Fixed] Record a source that stopped where the screen for it can see. A provider reported as unavailable without a cooldown of its own wrote nothing at all, so Search said unavailable while Advanced and the support bundle said nothing had happened. It is recorded once, by whichever of the job or the search actually observed it, and never with a deadline, because this failure names none.
- [Fixed] Hold one search to one budget. The retry against the install directory ran a second complete pass, so a source that never answers could hold a controller-visible search for twice as long, which is the multiplication the budget was added to stop. The fallback spends what is left of the same deadline.
- [Fixed] Treat a provider challenge as one at every page it owns, not only at the first. Skipped as merely "not 200", a provider challenging every page that matched returned an empty list, and an empty list read as a successful search of no results: the source was shown as healthy with nothing found, and its cached rows were not restored because nothing had failed. A challenge now stops that provider's remaining reads and reports it as unavailable, keeping whatever it returned first.
- [Fixed] Say a source is partial when it answered and then stopped. One screen could show downloadable rows from a source while the summary above them said that source had nothing.
- [Fixed] Record why a download ended as provider-unavailable. Both terminal paths returned before the ordinary bookkeeping, so failed downloads were not counted, the provider's own last error never described it, and the support bundle said a provider had been unavailable without saying why, which is the one question a bug report answered from that archive has to ask. The deadline the provider had already named is preserved rather than overwritten.
- [Fixed] Hold a URL known during a search to what the transport will structurally accept before promising a download from it. An absolute attachment link from another origin, and a GitHub URL carrying userinfo or a non-default port, were offered as directly downloadable and could only be refused after the user chose them.
- [Fixed] Require a provider's sitemap to be the document it was asked for, and every entry in it to be that provider's own. Matched by path shape alone, an entry from any origin became part of a cached index recorded as complete, and its origin was then discarded, so a later search matched a game that never existed there.
- [Fixed] Stop discarding a GitHub repository for saying "no recoil", which is an ordinary single-player table feature exactly like unlocking everything.
- [Fixed] Give a failed linked read an owner in the diagnostics. The source that linked it keeps its own row and therefore discards the failure, so a provider answering HTTP 500 for every repository another page named recorded nothing at all and looked healthy on the one screen that exists to say which source is failing. It is counted and named against the provider that answered, and stays narrower than a search failure: no search of it happened, its own state is not relabelled by an optional read, and no deadline is written.
- [Fixed] Read a page one source links at another on that provider's own terms. A repository or topic named by a Playground page was fetched without consulting the linked provider's cooldown, so a source deliberately waiting still received requests because something else pointed at it, and its refusals were attributed to nobody.
- [Fixed] Say which kind of wait a download is serving out. The panel described a provider refusing as this client's ordinary preparation countdown, and a rate limit a provider's own resolver handles internally was counted in no diagnostic at all.
- [Fixed] Stop offering a table this could never download. Three sources publish an exact byte count before selection, and a size already known to exceed the transfer ceiling was still presented as directly downloadable, so the refusal cost a press, a provider countdown and a wait to learn what the search already knew.
- [Security] Take a GitHub row's identity from the repository it is in, rather than reading it back out of a page URL the answer declared. A repository naming a page elsewhere gave its rows a `topic_id` and an `artifact_id` derived from that URL, so the identity the panel selects by and the key the result list dedupes on stopped naming the repository the table is actually in.
- [Security] Use a declared GitHub page only when it is one of GitHub's own. That URL is what a row shows as its source, what an imported table records as where it came from, and the Referer the artifact transfer names, and it was accepted as any public HTTPS address. A release asset served from somewhere else is dropped where it is read, rather than at the transfer where the host policy would refuse it after the user had already pressed. The committed path and object name of a table in a tree are also read whole or not at all, like every other field here that reaches a URL.
- [Fixed] Refuse a VGTimes sitemap that declares more game shards than can be read, instead of taking the first few. Trimming the list is the same defect as caching a crawl that lost a page: every game in the shards that were dropped disappears, the crawl still calls itself complete, and nothing says so.
- [Fixed] Never cache a sitemap crawl that lost a page as the complete index. Both new sitemap sources kept going when one declared page or shard failed and then wrote the subset down for six hours, so a single transient failure silently removed every game or post that page contained from every later search, with nothing saying so. A partial crawl still answers the search in front of the user; it is no longer recorded as the index.
- [Fixed] Read a VGTimes sitemap as a whole XML document rather than by pattern. A document that stopped early still yielded the entries that arrived, counted as read and would have been cached, so a partial index looked like a shorter one. Only the compressed stream was being checked for completeness.
- [Security] Read every GitHub field that ends up in a URL whole or not at all. A branch identity longer than the accepted bound was shortened first and validated second, so a 300 character name became a 255 character name nobody had ever used and the committed-table route was built on it. That is the same invented ref as the earlier fallback, arrived at by truncation. The repository identity and the page a row names as its source follow the same rule.
- [Fixed] Give each cached search row its own age. One answer routinely mixes freshness, because a search that one provider failed re-serves that provider's cached rows beside rows another provider has just returned, and a single timestamp for the file was wrong for half of it either way: refreshing it kept a dead source alive indefinitely, and preserving it aged a table fetched seconds ago to almost seven days.
- [Fixed] Stop discarding a GitHub repository for describing what an ordinary table does. Unlocking everything is a single-player table capability and was treated as proof of a multiplayer cheat, and bypassing counted on its own without naming what was being bypassed, so repositories were dropped before anything checked whether they even carry a table. Unambiguous multiplayer and anti-cheat techniques are still refused wherever they are found.
- [Fixed] Let The Cheat Script's two dates corroborate each other instead of one overruling the other. A current post carrying a stale or unreadable date was dropped as legacy even though the route the site itself files it under says otherwise; a post is now skipped only when everything known about it agrees it is out of range.
- [Security] Refuse a GitHub tree answer that does not declare itself complete. GitHub states this on every one of them, so a missing or differently typed flag is an answer that never claimed to be whole, and reading it as complete makes a repository whose committed table sits past the cut look like a repository without one.
- [Fixed] Stop inventing a GitHub default branch. A search answer naming no branch this understands used to acquire `main`, and the committed-table route then went looking on a ref nobody named. The repository keeps no branch instead, its releases still work, and the tree route is skipped and counted.
- [Fixed] Keep a provider cooldown that a search only observed. A search that found a provider already inside its cooldown recorded that skip as a fresh provider failure, and a failure carrying no status and no `Retry-After` writes a cleared deadline: one search during a ten minute cooldown made the next search eligible at once. Nothing is contacted, so nothing is recorded, and the source is still reported as unavailable for that query.
- [Fixed] Let a table the user selected be asked for. The download path consulted the cooldown the background listing crawl keeps and failed the acquisition outright, with a generic error, for a limit a search recorded and for a default minute the provider may never have asked for. Throttling a download somebody is watching belongs to the retry state machine that shows it as a countdown and honours the provider's own number.
- [Fixed] Keep a VGTimes download token across a rate limit. A limit answering the challenge sent the generic retry back to the start of the chain, which asks the waiting page for another token, and that request is the one telling the site a download is starting. It is waited out where the token still is, and a limit that outlasts the attempts leaves marked as one the retry layer must not simply run again.
- [Fixed] Answer only the VGTimes timing formula this client actually understands. Any expression merely beginning with `(function` was being answered with this client's own timing, so a different question would have received a confident stale answer instead of the adapter reporting that the contract moved. The operand that answer fills is bound to its own assignment rather than to the expression mentioning the page load time somewhere: matching the shape and finding that name elsewhere does not establish that the value in that position came from it.
- [Fixed] Refuse a VGTimes item page whose two download identities disagree. The route's own `lid` is the authority and the control's attribute corroborates it, and silently preferring the first left a rebuilt control looking readable.
- [Fixed] Refuse a VGTimes sitemap shard whose gzip stream stopped early. A truncated stream still yields whole entries up to the cut, so the crawl counted it as read and cached a partial game index for hours. A shard that did not finish is not a smaller shard.
- [Fixed] Stop dropping a GitHub search for a title that is not spelled in Latin. Queries were built from ASCII letters and digits only, so a Cyrillic or CJK library entry produced no query at all and the provider silently disappeared for exactly the names a user is least able to retype.
- [Fixed] Stop treating a missing post date as an unavailable artifact on The Cheat Script. `lastmod` is optional description, and a post declaring none, or one that could not be read, was dropped before its page could say what it hosts. The year now comes from the validated post path in that case, so the known-old optimization survives without an optional field deciding availability.
- [Fixed] Restore a cached search row with the shape it was saved with. A field added to the record in this version shifted a positional reconstruction, so every row restored from cache claimed it needed an archive password and stopped being marked stale.
- [Fixed] Let stale search results actually expire. Re-serving cached rows for a provider that failed wrote them back with a fresh timestamp, so repeated failed searches restarted the seven day horizon indefinitely and a source that stayed down was kept alive by the searches that could not reach it.
- [Security] Refuse a GitHub repository identity that is a pair of path segments rather than a pair of names. One of the two GitHub routes takes that identity from a link on another provider's page, which is third-party text, and `.` and `..` passed the check that exists to prevent exactly this: `github.com/../../x` built an API path that resolves to a different route once the URL is normalized. A committed table's path is held to the same rule.
- [Fixed] Say a cancelled download was cancelled. An acquisition waiting behind another one of the same provider had not reached the worker that records its own cancellation, so pressing Cancel reported "the download stopped without reporting an outcome", which reads as a failure of something the user had just stopped on purpose.
- [Fixed] Expand a compressed VGTimes sitemap within the limit its transfer was given, instead of expanding all of it and measuring afterwards. Four hundred kilobytes on the wire allocated four hundred megabytes before anything refused it, on a device where that matters.
- [Fixed] Keep the entry URL a VGTimes listing published rather than rebuilding one around its numeric id. The site resolves an entry by that id whatever name follows it, so the placeholder worked, but this is the source page a result shows and the origin an imported table records, and provenance that does not match the page it was read from cannot be followed back.
- [Changed] Solve the VGTimes challenge off the backend's event loop. It is arithmetic rather than a request, and it took the only thread for a fifth of a second while the panel was polling it.
- [New] Added VGTimes as an anonymous searchable provider. The game is its own path segment there and tables have their own listing, so discovery reads the site's declared sitemap once, ranks it locally, and then reads only table listings: no entry has to be opened to find out that it is a trainer or a save editor. Each item page publishes an exact byte count and links a report carrying the artifact's SHA-256, so the digest is known before the row is chosen, and the exact filename the site will serve is read rather than derived because the site also serves archives CE Decky cannot import.
- [New] VGTimes downloads happen in the plugin instead of asking anyone to drive a web page. The site's guest chain is a waiting page, a countdown it enforces rather than draws, and a challenge whose answer is arithmetic over data the server itself supplies. Every route, request field, wait and refusal code is read from the site's own page and client module rather than frozen in CE Decky, so a site that moves makes this say it no longer understands the contract instead of sending a confident wrong answer. Nothing from the site is executed, and the one slot the site's own script fills with an automation probe is sent empty: CE Decky is not a browser, and no code path here can claim otherwise.
- [New] A provider that serves one download at a time is waited for rather than argued with. VGTimes tells a guest, in its own words, that only one file may be downloaded at once; CE Decky now holds a single download in flight for a provider that declares this, retries a bounded number of times reusing the token it already has, and if it never clears, reports the sentence the site would have shown rather than a number.
- [New] GitHub answers a table search of its own. It was registered as a source that only resolved a repository another catalog had already linked, so a game whose table is published on GitHub and nowhere else could not be found at all. A search now queries the repository index, spends a bounded slice of the anonymous budget GitHub reports back, ranks a repository on its name with the words of the query removed, drops Cheat Engine itself, general table dumps and anything naming multiplayer cheat techniques, and offers a repository only when it actually carries a table rather than a README promising one. Releases are read first and the tree only when they carry none.
- [New] A table committed in a repository tree is downloadable, and verified. That route advertises git's own object name rather than a content digest, so the downloaded bytes are hashed the way git defines the name and must reproduce it exactly. It is never recorded or presented as a SHA-256.
- [Changed] An exhausted anonymous GitHub budget is a wait rather than a broken source. GitHub reports it as HTTP 403 with a reset timestamp instead of the usual 429 and `Retry-After`, which was read as a failure, so the source was reported as unavailable when it had only been asked too often.
- [New] Added The Cheat Script as an anonymous searchable provider. Discovery reads only the site's declared sitemap and a bounded set of locally ranked posts, while current Yandex Disk artifacts are described through its public API so their exact filename, byte count and advertised SHA-256 are known before selection. Acquisition requests the transient download URL only when the user starts it, keeps that URL backend-only, and passes the bytes through the existing host, redirect, size, digest, archive and table validation boundaries.

## 0.9.11 — 2026-09-03

- [Fixed] Let later FearLess listing pages reuse the stride page one already proved when phpBB renders the current page as a non-link. A later page may provide no independent stride, but its page number and totals must still fit the established geometry, and any explicit contradiction is refused without letting that page redefine the index.
- [Fixed] Keep the FearLess index complete when its listing has one page, sparse pagination, or changes page size. A page step now comes only from an explicit page-number/offset relation that agrees with the advertised page and topic totals; an ambiguous relation is refused, a one-page index persists without a meaningless step, and the search that discovers a resize immediately refreshes the next page on the new boundary.
- [Fixed] Mark persisted diagnostics from a removed provider as historical instead of presenting its old failure as the state of a provider the current registry can still use.
- [Fixed] Make the live provider route probe follow FearLess's declared page size and fetch an actual topic page instead of assuming fifty rows and fetching a second listing in its place.
- [Changed] Open Cheat Tables is removed. It has been unreachable since a site-wide Cloudflare challenge went up in front of every route CE Decky needs, measured with this plugin's own network client: only `robots.txt` and `sitemap.xml` answer, and the readable half carries no titles to build an index from. The route the adapter used to search it was also one that site's own `robots.txt` disallows, which this project does not do, so the generic phpBB search job went with the provider. A table already imported from it keeps working and still names where it came from.
- [Fixed] Read how big a FearLess listing page is, instead of assuming fifty. That number is what every stored offset in the background index means. A forum that changed it would have left the crawl walking every other page, holding an index with holes in it that reported itself complete and rejecting the offsets it had already saved, which is the kind of wrong answer nothing would have reported. It now comes from the listing's own pagination links, is stored beside the offsets it explains, and an index whose page size no longer matches the listing is dropped and rebuilt rather than reinterpreted.
- [Changed] A provider says where it lives in one place. Its base URL is part of the provider descriptor now, so the origin of every route is declared once instead of being retyped at each call site that builds one.

## 0.9.10 — 2026-09-03

- [Fixed] Offer to stop a table activation only while there is a Cheat Engine to stop, and let stopping it end the activation. The stop this version added was offered for the whole of **Use this table**, including the durable profile and consent writes that run before anything is started, where pressing it could only answer that there was nothing to stop; and after Cheat Engine had connected it said the same thing while the Cheat Engine it had started was running and stoppable. The button is now on screen throughout and pressable exactly for the steps that own one, which are the launch, the attach and the first live read, and it stops that exact Cheat Engine. Stopping it also ends the run: the live read after a connection swallows a failed query on purpose, so an activation the user had just stopped went on to report a loaded table and a connected Cheat Engine that were no longer there.
- [Fixed] Say what a window over the game is, instead of counting the sweeps that failed to take it down. **Advanced** reported "N Cheat Engine window(s) could not be hidden" from a counter the suppression sweep raises once per timer tick, so one window nothing can hide became twenty windows after twenty seconds, and the number never came back down once the window did. The bridge now publishes both halves: how many attempts failed, which is the diagnostic a bug report needs, and whether a window is over the game right now, which is what the panel says and the only one of the two that can say the screen came back.
- [Fixed] Tell two identical-looking dropdown choices apart. Cleaning an invisible character out of a label is what keeps a table with one decorated line usable, but two lines whose labels differ only in what was removed then rendered as the same controller choice, with nothing on screen to say which value each would write. A colliding label now carries its own value, and the value carried alongside it is untouched.
- [Fixed] Count the dropdown values a table actually lost. The marker was raised as soon as the accepted list reached its ceiling, before anything proved another value existed, and one marker stood for however long the tail was: a list ending exactly on the limit was reported as having lost a value, and a list that lost a thousand was reported as having lost one. **Review -> Contents** presents this as an exact count, so it is now exactly that.
- [Fixed] Decide what a table can execute in one place. The import path treated any `<LuaScript>` element as executable content and the Review inspector required the script to have something in it, so **Review** could say **No static executable-content markers found** directly above **Confirmation required**, which is the one decision this project asks the user to make. Both now read the same classifier: a script with a body, an Auto Assembler script with a body, a window the table carries, and the embedded files themselves rather than the container holding them.
- [Fixed] Stop advertising GitHub as a source a search will query. GitHub Releases is reached only when another catalog exposes an exact repository link, which is what `DESIGN.md` has always said and what the adapter does, but the registry declared it enabled and ready while the search scheduled three hard-coded jobs and summarized the same three, so it could be offered as a source that would never return a row. The registry now says how each provider is reached, and the search's jobs, its per-source summary and the query plan the panel shows are all built from that one answer. A provider declared searchable with no adapter behind it is reported as a failed source rather than quietly left out, which is how this divergence stayed invisible.
- [Limitation] A standalone trainer file stays unsupported, on purpose. A `.CETRAINER` is a table packaged to run by itself, and the format exists precisely to execute what it carries without it being read first; CE Decky's whole trust boundary is the opposite, that the user sees a table's contents and authorizes those exact bytes. Four of the 86 tables sampled from FearLess arrived as archives holding no `.CT`, which is where a user meets this, so `README.md` now says it in their terms rather than leaving the import to fail without a reason they can act on. What those four archives actually hold was not established; only that none holds a `.CT`.
- [Fixed] Stop losing a whole table to one label. A cheat description carrying an invisible or bidirectional character was refused, and so was a dropdown line whose value the runtime cannot send, and in both cases the refusal cost the user every cheat in the table. Measured across 86 tables sampled from FearLess across three years of the forum: four were refused this way, and all four are perfectly good tables. The protection is unchanged, because the character is what was dangerous and the character is what goes: a label that would visually spoof a filename or a process now has that character removed rather than the table thrown away, an over-long value costs that value, and a label past the display ceiling is cut to it. A process hint is the exception and is dropped rather than cleaned up, because that one is offered as the executable to attach to and a cleaned-up spelling is the name it was made to imitate. What was removed is counted rather than swallowed: **Review -> Contents** says how many labels had hidden characters taken out and how many values could not be carried, and both reach the plugin log and the support bundle.
- [Security] Say when a table brings a window of its own. `<Forms>` is a section Cheat Engine acts on: it builds the designed window when the table is opened, and that window's controls carry Lua event handlers. A table whose only executable content is one carries no `<LuaScript>`, so it was imported as having no executable content at all and Review told the user exactly that, which is the one thing this project asks them to decide about. Such a table now reports its own window in **Review -> Executable content**, and the confirmation names it alongside Lua, Auto Assembler and embedded content. Nothing else changes: exact-SHA consent was already required for every table, and the window itself is taken off the game by the sweep above.
- [Fixed] Take a form the table's own Lua script created off the running game. Executing that script is what this version started doing, so a table can now build a window of its own, and Cheat Engine's `hideAllCEWindows` does not reach one: measured on this device, a 300x120 trainer form reported itself visible both before and after that call, stayed mapped over Half-Life 2 for the whole session, and went down the moment its `Visible` property was assigned false. The sweep already enumerates that form to decide whether anything is on screen, so it now hides what it can itself see, and the same table leaves nothing mapped over the game. The suppression count was the reason nothing could have been diagnosed from a bug report: it counted a window seen and a hide that did not raise, which for this window was neither, so it climbed once a second for the whole session while the window it was counting never moved. It now counts windows actually taken down, and a window the sweep could not put down is reported separately: **Advanced** says outright that a window is over the game and that stopping Cheat Engine gets the screen back, which is the only one of these the user can see for themselves.
- [Fixed] Read the Playground download diagnostic off the record the parser actually produces. The provider probe still asked an `ArtifactRecord` for the field this version renamed, so **Advanced -> Provider diagnostics**' one download check would have ended in an attribute error the next time it was run. Nothing exercised it; it is covered by a regression now.
- [Fixed] Run the table's own Lua script instead of letting Cheat Engine ask about it where nobody can answer. A table that carries a Lua script is the ordinary case, not an exotic one, and Cheat Engine asks before it runs one. The question is a modal form of its own: it stops the resident bridge inside the table load, before the bridge has published anything at all, and the sweep that takes Cheat Engine's windows off a running game will not close it, because closing one of Cheat Engine's own forms is not that sweep's job. Gamescope shows the game, so the question is not on screen either. Observed on this device with Half-Life 2: pressing **Use this table** on a FearLess table went quiet for five minutes and ended in "the resident Cheat Engine bridge did not report a heartbeat in time", with Cheat Engine running the whole time and the window "Execute this lua script?" sitting behind the game. The same table with its script removed connected in six seconds. The user has already authorized this exact table SHA for execution in Review, before Cheat Engine is started, so the bridge now loads the table through Cheat Engine's own stream form, which takes that decision as a parameter: the script runs and the question is never raised. It is the same answer for every table and every game, and which route opened a table is published in the status, the plugin log and the support bundle, so a table that ran code can be established afterwards. A Cheat Engine that offers no such form still loads tables the old way, and says so.
- [Fixed] Publish a heartbeat before opening the table, so a Cheat Engine that stops inside one of its own windows is a reported state rather than silence. Opening a table is the one step of the bridge's startup that runs code Cheat Engine and the table brought with them, and everything that could have made a stop visible only started once that startup had finished. The bridge now publishes liveness and starts its window sweep before the load, and marks that heartbeat as starting rather than ready, so a bridge that is alive but has no table open is never reported as a connected session. A launch that runs out its budget now names where it stopped, including the last Cheat Engine window it answered.
- [Changed] Say what a table activation is doing, and offer to stop it. Activating a table is several minutes in the worst case, almost all of it spent waiting for Cheat Engine to open the table and answer, and the Review screen greyed every control for the duration with no other sign of life, which is indistinguishable from a panel that has hung. It now names the step it is on and how long it has been on it, and while a Cheat Engine is actually starting it offers the same stop Home does, instead of a Cancel that cannot be pressed.
- [Security] Refusing a source because it defends itself against automation now carries the same evidence burden as using one: the exact control on the exact route must be named, and the ordinary explanations ruled out by test, because analytics also collects fingerprints and a legacy URL redirects everyone.
- [Security] `robots.txt` is honoured as the crawling directive it is. Discovery crawls only a site's declared sitemap and its allowed listings and never a disallowed search, feed, pagination or attachment-enumeration route, while retrieving one artifact the user explicitly chose is treated as the user's own retrieval rather than index building. Host policy, HTTPS, redirect revalidation, public-address checks, digest verification and execution consent are unchanged, and a source that requires an account or answers with a bot challenge stays refused.
- [Changed] A provider whose download link must be fetched rather than stored now owns that exchange itself. `ArtifactRecord` carries one acquisition slot instead of a field per provider, and the download resolver asks the request to resolve itself instead of branching per provider, so cooldown and artifact host policy come from the request rather than from a hard-coded Playground path.
- [Changed] Provider identity is declared once. A provider's display name, rank, host policy and adapter now live in a single descriptor instead of being repeated across the definition list, the host map, the search job list and the browser-handoff table, and search results are attributed by provider rather than by list position.

## 0.9.9 — 2026-09-02

- [Fixed] Stop offering a table once the user has stopped using it. Answering "Stop using it" withdrew the table's execution authorization and left the table selected, so Home kept its row, its **Load table & start CE** and its **Configure cheats**: the panel a working table has, for one that had just been proven not to work, and the only sign anything had happened was a toast. The selection is now cleared as well, and the game returns to having no table selected. Nothing is destroyed by it: the bytes stay in the game's library, and the table's pinned controls, saved values and startup actions are archived against its exact SHA, so clearing the mark under **Advanced** and selecting it again brings all of it back. The clearing happens after the withdrawal rather than before it, so the authorization archived with those preferences is the withdrawn one, and a failure to clear says the withdrawal itself is already saved and stays saved. Switching that table's Auto-load off is cleanup that follows the withdrawal too, rather than running ahead of it: it used to go first, so any failure of it ended the whole answer before anything was withdrawn, leaving the worst state available, a table marked as not working so search stops offering it and still selected and still authorized to execute. A failure there is now reported alongside the stop that did happen and names the arming that is still on.
- [Fixed] Re-read the profile whenever the quick-access panel is shown again. Work started by a press outlives the press, and it can outlive the panel: Steam recreates this browser view when a game returns to the foreground, which is exactly what stopping Cheat Engine does. On this device the replacement panel read the profile 340 ms before the withdrawal of an authorization landed, and nothing polls that state, so it went on showing an execution authorization that no longer existed, with **Load table & start CE** enabled for it, until the plugin was reloaded. The panel now reads the authority and the record of tables that did not work every time it comes back into view. Every durable write still refreshes on its own path; this only closes the window where the panel that receives the answer is not the panel that asked. A panel created while the quick-access panel is already on screen re-reads as soon as its first status arrives, because that panel never sees a visibility change at all: the loader starts it at visible, so its own first read was the only read it would ever make, and a write that landed a moment later stayed invisible for the rest of the session. Reading again the instant the first status arrives is not enough on its own, because that second read goes out one round trip after the first and the write it is racing landed 340 ms behind them both, so one further bounded read follows past the far side of that window. It happens once per panel and is cancelled if the panel is hidden or dismounted first; nothing repeats it, and nothing polls. Loaders too old to say whether the panel is visible keep the previous behavior instead of failing.
- [Changed] Let an agent that has no Git transport read the repository. The rule against synthesizing a partial worktree was written against inventing a way to publish, and it also forbade the only way a review running in the cloud can reach this branch at all: it has no ordinary checkout and fetches files one at a time through a plugin. The prohibition now covers what it was meant to cover, committing, pushing, tagging or releasing from a tree that was assembled rather than checked out, and says plainly that assembling a read-only copy of one exact commit to read is allowed, provided the copy names its commit, is not treated as a checkout, and reports every path that could not be fetched.
- [Fixed] Install onto a Decky Loader that pushes its install prompt in the newer event envelope. v3.2.6 sent that event as message type 3 and v3.2.8-pre1 sends the identical payload as type 5, and the prompt carries the only confirmation id there is, so the developer install helper did not fail loudly: it waited for a message that had already gone past until Decky closed the socket, and nothing was installed. Both envelopes are now recognised as the one event, and everything about the prompt is still validated before it is confirmed.
- [Fixed] Wait out a provider rate limit instead of failing the download. On this device the same table refused with HTTP 429 at 20:03:07 and downloaded normally at 20:03:35, because a background listing crawl and the download were asking FearLess for the same budget within five seconds of each other; the user's own answer was to press the row again, which is what this does, only sooner and without having to guess that it was worth trying. A rate-limited download now returns to the cancellable waiting state the acquisition screen already has, says the provider is rate limiting rather than preparing anything, and retries a bounded number of times: the provider's own `Retry-After` when it names one, and a short interactive backoff when it does not. It does not adopt the background index's cooldown, whose default minute is longer than the throttle actually observed, and a throttle that does not clear still ends as a bounded failure that names the status. The wait covers the request that asks a provider for a transient link as well as the transfer itself, so the phase before the download is no longer the one place a 429 still meant "failed, press the row again later"; the single exception is a limit that arrives on Playground's lock exchange, once its one-shot token has been issued and its countdown served, where asking again would replay a handshake the provider has already closed. A rate limit that was waited out and recovered from is now counted in **Advanced → Provider diagnostics** with the wait it cost, apart from failed downloads and from the cooldown a terminal 429 persists, so a provider that throttles every transfer no longer looks exactly like one that serves files immediately.
- [Fixed] Name the provider a download is actually coming from. The acquisition screen was titled "Download table from Playground" whatever provider was serving the file, and every download of this session came from FearLess. The title and the waiting line both use the provider on the artifact now.
- [Changed] Say where a table is marked as not working, outside the search screen. Keeping a table the refusal dialog asked about is a real answer, and the same bytes can be marked while another game was playing them, so a marked table can legitimately still be the selected one. Search greyed those rows and nothing else mentioned them: Home's table row and the list of tables already imported for this game both presented one exactly like a table that works, and that list is the shortest route back to the table that has just failed. Both now say the table is marked as not working. It stays selectable and stays runnable, because the record is advice and a refusal to re-import, never a trust decision.

## 0.9.8 — 2026-09-02

- [Fixed] Read Cheat Engine's own module list before the table, not after it. It is Cheat Engine's own list only while Cheat Engine has no process open, and that is the one moment the two calls that bring a game back can be found without its symbol table. Loading a table is not inert: Cheat Engine runs the table's own hooks, and a table's Lua script can open a process by itself, which would have left that reading the game's modules at the game's addresses or refusing to read any. Nothing that can open a process now runs before it. A module list that comes back empty, or longer than this walks, is also no longer recorded as the module being absent, because neither is an answer about what is in it. An empty one is now waited on rather than crossed: attaching is what makes that list the game's, so for a bounded couple of seconds the bridge reads it again before attaching, and then attaches regardless. Loading the table waits with it, because loading a table is one of the things that can open a process, so holding back only the bridge's own attach would have let the table cross that line first. A list that answered is not waited on at all, so a Cheat Engine that was ready loses nothing.
- [Fixed] Give asking a game back and telling it that it is active their own budgets. The request is posted in one sweep and the window reports itself back in a later one, so a window restored by the last sweep of a single shared budget had no sweep left to be told anything in, and would have come back on screen and stayed in the slow inactive state the activation exists to leave. The reverse held too: a window that never came back, or that was destroyed and recreated under another handle, kept the sweep asking for the rest of the session. Each window asked back now waits for the restore to land on its own patience, and only starts spending its handful of activation attempts once the window reports itself back: a restore is posted rather than sent, and a game that is loading answers when it gets to it, so a game slower than the attempts would have come back on screen and stayed slow. A handle the game no longer owns is dropped, which is what bounds a window that never returns.
- [Fixed] Prove every table in a module's export directory lies inside that module before reading it. The addresses come out of the image's own headers, and this reads Cheat Engine's own memory, where an address that leaves the image does not fail: it returns some other module's bytes. The export directory, the name, ordinal and address tables and each export name are now required to end inside the image and not merely to start there, the header fields that say how long the header is and how many data directories it carries are required to cover the one being read, and a name that runs to the last byte without ending is refused. Nothing changed about what resolves on a real module; what changed is what an unexpected one can do.
- [Changed] Let the export in the module answer for both halves of the restore, not one. The lookup that reads a name out of the module needs no symbol table, and the question half already used it, but the half that posts the request refused before trying when Cheat Engine offered no symbol lookup at all. It now asks for the address the same way the other half does, and what the resolver said when it could not is kept, so a status that stops at the post half says why instead of only where.
- [Fixed] Say that the restore capability is still settling, on the screen that exists to say it. Two of its states are Cheat Engine still starting rather than a refusal, and both publish no minimized-state query at all while they hold, which is exactly what **Advanced** was keying the row on: the two labels written for those states could never appear. The row now follows the capability itself and says the wait usually settles by itself.
- [Fixed] Keep a cleared table cleared when the list cannot be re-read afterwards. The record is edited durably and then re-read, and that re-read was neither awaited nor caught: a failure left the screen showing the entry as though the press had done nothing, and raised an unhandled rejection. Two quick clears could also leave whichever read landed last on screen, including an older one. The re-read now cannot reject, only the newest one is allowed to repaint, and a failed one says the list may be out of date and that the change was saved.
- [Changed] Say in the README what CE Decky will not do, instead of what had not been retested yet. Three of the listed limitations were not limitations: live cheat control and table search are confirmed working on the device, and a provider that refuses anonymous downloads is already reported as unavailable on the search screen itself, where it is useful. What replaces them is what a user actually runs into: Cheat Engine's own interface is never shown, so there is no scanning and no writing a table; a table too large to read back is configured rather than switched live; a game with no Windows build has nothing to attach to until a Proton tool is forced for it; an encrypted archive is refused; starting Cheat Engine can push a game aside for a moment; and running Cheat Engine costs what it costs. The rest of the page caught up with the panel: the record of tables that did not work has its own review screen, **Processes** is described including the case it exists for, and auto-load switching itself off with the last saved cheat is written down.
- [Fixed] Tell a game that has been asked back that it is active again. Coming back from minimized is not the same thing, and nothing else was going to say it: Cheat Engine takes the foreground as it starts, the game is deactivated and minimizes itself, and CE Decky then hides every Cheat Engine window, which leaves the session with no foreground window at all. The restore put the window back on screen with nothing to reactivate it, so the game went on running the loop it uses while it is not the active application. On this device Half-Life 2 came back at 18 frames a second instead of 60, stayed there for the rest of the session, and stayed there after Cheat Engine had exited, which made it look like Cheat Engine was still costing something when it was long gone. The window that was asked back is now told its application is active, once it reports itself no longer minimized so the game has already handled the restore. Measured on the target: 60 frames a second before, one brief dip while Cheat Engine starts, and 60 afterwards, including after Cheat Engine exits. Only a window this session actually asked back is told anything, and **Advanced** and the support bundle now carry both halves, because a game that came back and stayed slow is missing exactly the second one.
- [Changed] Require what the campaign learned to be preserved before any of it is deleted. The pre-publication cleanup removes the documents this project keeps most of its knowledge of its target in, and almost none of that is derivable from the code: it was paid for with device sessions. `AGENTS.md` now makes migration a prerequisite for deletion rather than a footnote to it, names what has to survive, including measurements with the hardware and method behind them, the games and Proton builds a behavior was and was not seen on, and work that is owed rather than done, and says to create a durable document when the permanent set has no place for a subject instead of dropping the finding.
- [Limitation] Say what running Cheat Engine beside a game costs.
