Measured on a Steam Machine with a demanding game holding a steady 38 fps: attaching Cheat Engine costs about 6% of one core, roughly a sixth of which is CE Decky's own resident bridge and the rest Cheat Engine simply being open, and the frame rate did not move by more than two identical runs differ from each other. A Steam Deck has fewer and slower cores and spends one power budget on both the processor and the graphics, so the same work costs more there and comes out of frames rather than out of spare capacity. That measurement has not been made yet, and the README says which machine the figure is from rather than presenting it as the general case.
- [Fixed] Bring back a game that Cheat Engine pushed aside. Asking a window back needs two calls into Windows, one that says whether it really is minimized and one that posts the request, and Cheat Engine's own symbol table answers nothing at all for either of them in a Cheat Engine started inside the game's own Wine session, which is the only place CE Decky ever starts one. Not `IsIconic`, not `PostMessageW`, not `user32.dll`, not even Cheat Engine's own executable, from the first moment of the session to the last. The same Cheat Engine, the same table and the same Proton, given a Wine session of its own, resolve every one of them, which is what made this take three sittings to find. The module list beside that table is complete and correct, so the two addresses are now read out of `user32.dll`'s own loaded image instead: bounded reads of Cheat Engine's own memory, both image signatures checked, and the exact export name found in the module's own sorted name table. That list is read before the game is attached, because afterwards it is the game's list, and an address out of the game's memory called inside Cheat Engine is the exact mistake this whole path exists to avoid. Nothing is called by name, and the answer is still proven against Cheat Engine's own hidden window before any game window is asked anything.
- [Fixed] Find the game's windows when nothing is in the foreground. They are found by walking the system's own chain of top-level windows, and a chain has to be entered from a window. That was the foreground window, and CE Decky keeps every Cheat Engine window hidden, so there is no foreground window to enter from: on the device it answered zero for whole sessions, the chain was never walked, and no window was ever found to belong to the game. Even with everything else working the game would have been left dark. Cheat Engine's own main window is top-level whether or not it is visible, and that is what the walk starts from now.
- [Fixed] Run one resident bridge inside a Cheat Engine rather than two. The private runtime loads it from `main.lua` so that it is running before Cheat Engine has enumerated its own autorun directory, and the file sits in that directory, so Cheat Engine loaded it again a moment later. Both copies were live in one Lua state, answering the same commands and writing the same heartbeat over each other. The second copy starts after the game has been attached, which is the one moment Cheat Engine's module list is no longer its own, so it is the copy that can establish least and it was the one writing last: a session that could ask the game back reported that it could not.
- [Fixed] Treat a symbol that is not there yet as not there yet. Both calls were looked for once, from the bridge's first lines, before Cheat Engine had finished starting, and whatever that moment said was kept as the session's answer: every sweep afterwards read the cached refusal rather than asking again. A name that does not resolve is now asked again on every window sweep for twenty seconds, the one symbol table reload is kept back until waiting alone has plainly not settled it, and the sweeps that ask the game back no longer drain while there is still nothing to ask with. **Advanced** says it is still settling instead of naming a refusal. A name that does resolve and then answers wrongly is still refused outright and at once, because that is the one failure that would drop a healthy game out of fullscreen.
- [Changed] Look a symbol up through the lookup Cheat Engine's own scripts use. Cheat Engine has two entry points into its own symbol table and they reach different resolvers; the one Cheat Engine's own scripts rely on when they call a `user32` predicate by name is the one asked now, bare export names first, and a table that had nothing to answer with is reloaded once per session rather than every second. Nothing is executed by name either way: only a resolved address is ever called. It changed nothing on the target, because neither entry point answers there, and asking the right one is what established that.
- [Fixed] Never call into Cheat Engine's own process by a name it has not resolved. A local call runs code inside Cheat Engine itself, and a name it cannot resolve does not fail politely: on the device it took Cheat Engine down with an exception and cost the session. The address is looked up first and only a resolved one is ever called, so a missing symbol is now a reported refusal instead of a crash.
- [Fixed] Ask the minimized question the other way too. Cheat Engine offers two forms of the call that runs a function inside its own process, and its own shipped scripts use the one-parameter form for exactly this shape of call, a window handle into a `user32` predicate. CE Decky only ever tried the many-parameter form, which on this device answered nothing at all for `IsIconic`, so the game was never asked to come back. Both are tried now, the pair that answered is the one used afterwards, and what a refused call actually said is recorded instead of being swallowed. Posting the request still needs the many-parameter form, because it carries four parameters, and that is reported as its own half rather than as an unanswerable question.
- [Fixed] Say which half stopped a game being brought back. Cheat Engine takes the foreground when it starts and the game minimizes itself, and asking it back needs two calls: one that says whether a window really is minimized, and one that posts the request. Either can be missing and the game stays dark either way, but the panel reported both as "cannot tell whether the game is minimized" - including the case where that question is answered perfectly well and it is the request that cannot be sent, which pointed a bug report at a call that works. The bridge now records where it actually stopped, the panel names it, and it is logged and collected with the rest of the session.
- [Changed] Stop drawing a separator under every panel row. Steam draws one edge to edge, which is the same shape as the rule beside a section heading, so CE Decky had been pulling it in to 15% on either side; what that left was a short stripe floating clear of both edges and of the text it belonged to, reading as a stray mark rather than a division. Row spacing and the section rules already separate the panel. One constant in the panel's stylesheet turns the inset stripe back on.
- [Changed] Give the record of tables that did not work a screen of its own. It grows on its own and holds up to 512 entries, and it sat as a column of rows in the middle of Advanced that everything below it, including the plugin's own data and removal controls, had to be scrolled past. The panel now carries one line saying how many tables are refused and when the newest was recorded, and **Review** opens the list. Entries past the twelfth are reachable for the first time: the panel capped its list there and named the remainder, so the only way to clear an older one was to empty the whole record, which also re-offered every download that was never a table at all. The review screen pages through all of them instead, twenty-five at a time. A record that cannot be read still says so on the panel itself, because that is the state in which nothing is being refused.

## 0.9.7 — 2026-09-01

- [Fixed] Let a table that has already been seen on eight posts learn a ninth. The record keeps a bounded list of where its bytes were offered from, and it kept the eight it met first, so the post offering them right now was the one dropped and stayed unrecognisable in search. The oldest makes room instead.
- [Fixed] Stop counting a store's own library entry as a running game. It is dropped when working out which game you are playing, but that happened after the count everything else reads had been published, so Home could say two games were running when one was and the picker offered the launcher as one of the games you might have meant. Working out which entries are real now happens before any of it is published, so the raw observation never becomes something you can act on.
- [Fixed] Learn which post a table came from when a download is refused for bytes already known bad. Those bytes can have been recorded before anything knew where they came from, and the refusal is exactly the moment it is known, so the same archive was found, downloaded and refused again on every search. The recorded reason and its date are left as they were.
- [Changed] Show the whole search status instead of cutting it off. The per-source tally is the half that says a source returned nothing, and it was the half that fell off the end of the line: the row read "FearLess: 20 …". It wraps to a second line when there is a second line's worth to say, and the longer explanation stays behind that row's own help button.
- [Fixed] Take a support bundle's name in one step instead of two. Asking whether a name is free and then using it are separate moments, and two presses inside the same second ran them interleaved: both saw it free, both wrote, and one archive replaced the other. The name is now claimed outright, which also means an existing file at that name is stepped over rather than written through.
- [Fixed] Remember which post a bad table inside a downloaded archive came from. The file is unpacked, found not to be a table and deleted inside the import, so nothing afterwards could say where it came from, and a source almost never advertises the contents of a file inside an archive. The same archive was found, downloaded and refused again on every search.
- [Changed] Say which games are running when more than one is. That is the one thing CE Decky cannot work out for itself, and it asked by handing over the whole installed library with the first entry already chosen, so the two games it was asking about were indistinguishable from everything else and an arbitrary answer was one press away. They are listed first and marked, nothing is preselected, and the rest of the library is still there underneath. A single running game is still detected without asking.
- [Security] Redact a secret-shaped field on the way into the support bundle, not only on the way into the panel's own record. The panel already does it, but an RPC argument is a trust boundary whatever is expected on the other side of it, and the cost of being wrong is a credential inside a file attached to a public issue.
- [Fixed] Let two support bundles collected in the same second both survive. The name carries a whole second and every writer built in the same staging file, so one could truncate and delete the archive another was still writing. Each takes a free name and a staging file of its own.
- [Fixed] Keep the current settings, session records and cheat tables in a support bundle collected after a noisy day. Log tails can ask for far more of the one size budget than everything else together, and they were collected first, so exactly the evidence a failure needs could be crowded out by the history of one that was already fixed.
- [Security] Refuse to read a cheat table for the bundle through a symlinked directory inside the managed table tree. Only the file itself was protected; a redirected parent could have taken the read anywhere.
- [Fixed] Stop counting a trimmed log as something the bundle could not collect. A truncated file is in the archive and readable, and reporting it as missing made a healthy bundle look damaged.
- [Fixed] Let a local archive with one encrypted file reach its password prompt. That single-file shortcut exists because a provider download may have a password published beside it, and a file you picked yourself never does, so the import went ahead with no password and reported the refusal. One encrypted 7z now reaches the explanation of why it cannot be used at all.
- [Fixed] Stop offering live control for a table with more switchable cheats than can be read back. Apply changes what you asked for and then re-reads the whole table to confirm, and both exceed the same limit, so a change that had actually worked came back as a partial or unknown result. Such a table is now edited as stored configuration, and says so without claiming Cheat Engine is not running, which it is.
- [Fixed] Say that a game's selected table file is gone instead of showing it as no table selected. The profile, its authorization and its saved cheats were all still there, and the reason it would not start was on a screen there was no cause to open. The list of tables you already have is offered whenever there is one left, rather than only when there are two.
- [New] Look for the game's processes again from **Review cheat table**. The list is taken before that screen opens, so its own advice to start the game changed nothing on it.
- [Changed] Say so before a Wine or Proton helper is saved as a game's target process under **Advanced**. It stays selectable, because for an unusual game one of them really is the executable that runs it.
- [Fixed] Make **Retry** visibly do something. The search screen is opened as a detached window and never sees its props change, so it went on greying the rows it had just cleared and going on counting them: the press looked like it had done nothing at all. It re-reads the record now, and so does a download that turns out not to be a table, which is recorded while that screen is open.
- [Fixed] Remember which post a download that was not a table came from. Those bytes never enter the table library, so nothing could look that up afterwards and the row was offered, paid for with another provider countdown, downloaded and refused at import all over again. **Retry** frees such a row for good now instead of for one press.
- [Fixed] Ask before recording that a table did not work. The mark was written the moment a cheat refused, and the question came afterwards: answering **Keep it** left the table you had just chosen to go on using greyed out in search, refused on re-import, and listed under **Advanced** as not working. Nothing durable is written now until you answer, so **Keep it** changes nothing at all, and neither does dismissing the question with the controller's own Back button. **Stop using it** is what records it.
- [Changed] Give the search screen its results back. Search, the retry press and two lines of counts were four rows of controls above the list the screen exists to show, on a panel that fits about six results. They are one line now: the counts and the per-source tally on the left, small buttons on the right, in the same shape **Advanced** already uses, with when it was searched and what Retry does behind that row's own `?`. Retry appears only while a row on the page you are looking at is marked, counts exactly those rows, and clears exactly them.
- [Fixed] Keep a table that did not work marked in search. The mark is recorded against the table's exact bytes, and a search result only sometimes advertises what its bytes will be: the source most of these tables come from advertises nothing, so the mark was invisible on precisely the rows it had been recorded from. The same table was found, downloaded, reviewed and proved broken again on every search. Each record now also remembers the provider posts the bytes were downloaded from, which every result has, and those rows are greyed on sight.
- [Changed] Stop searching again from quietly unmarking a bad table. A download that turned out to be a damaged file was remembered only until the next search, and this screen searches by itself as soon as it is opened from the panel, so in practice the mark lasted until the next look at it. Searching again is how you look for a different table, not a claim that a broken one has been fixed.
- [New] Retry the marked tables from the screen that is showing them. **Search / Download** offers one press, naming how many of the rows in front of you are marked, that clears those marks and nothing else. A game update is the ordinary reason a table starts working again, and the alternative was **Advanced**, where the only control clears every game's records at once. It also frees a row retired because its download turned out not to be a table, which nothing else could clear.
- [New] Collect everything a bug report needs into one file. **Advanced -> Collect support bundle** writes a ZIP into your home folder and then tells you exactly where it is: CE Decky's backend log across every plugin load, what Proton and Cheat Engine printed for each game, the session records naming the exact table and target a Cheat Engine was given, your settings, game profiles and the tables they use, the provider diagnostics behind a search that found nothing, and what the panel itself did. Attaching it to an issue replaces the round of questions that used to come first. What could not be read is listed in the archive with the reason, because an unreadable state file is usually the thing being reported.
- [New] Say what the panel did. Nothing on the frontend was recorded anywhere, so a bug that happened on screen arrived as a screenshot and a sentence: which screen was open, what was pressed, what failed and what the backend answered all existed only until the panel closed. The panel now keeps a bounded record of that in memory and puts it in the support bundle, and a crash in the panel's own rendering, which used to leave no trace at all, is recorded before Decky's error screen replaces it.
- [New] Record what Cheat Engine reports, when it changes. The bridge's heartbeat says whether it is attached, to which process, whether it has the table, how a startup plan is progressing and what the last cheat it was asked to switch actually did. It was polled several times a second and written down never, so "the cheat did nothing" had no evidence behind it at all. Each change is now one line in the log, and an unchanged state stays silent.
- [New] Log what a table parsed as, what a game was configured with, and which cheats were switched. The log said a profile had been saved and not what was saved, said a table had been inspected and not what was in it, and said a batch of commands had been written and not what they were.
- [Changed] Say why a Cheat Engine launch failed in the log, not only in the panel. The reason lived in the operation the panel was polling and was gone the moment the panel was closed; the Proton build, prefix, display, table and target it was built from are recorded with it.
- [Changed] Redraw HexPaw. The previous artwork was clipped by its own canvas, with the tail cut off flat at the right edge and the ear tips at the top, and it carried a faint rectangular edge left over from how it was cut out. The new one sits whole inside the frame. The README picture and the panel's own inline copy come from the same file, and a check now fails if one is replaced without rebuilding the other.
- [Fixed] Let **Processes** be pressed when Cheat Engine is not running. Its whole purpose is picking a different process when the automatic choice was wrong, which is exactly the case where Cheat Engine will not start or will not attach, and it was disabled in precisely that situation. With a Cheat Engine running it still asks it which Windows processes it can see, so an exact PID can be attached to; without one it now lists the Windows executables the game's own processes are running, read from the system rather than from Cheat Engine, and one of those can be saved as the target for the next start.

## 0.9.6 — 2026-09-01

- [Fixed] Ask only a game that is actually minimized to come back. The request went out whenever the game was not the window in front, and that is not the same thing at all: a game behind the Steam overlay is running perfectly well, and for a window that is maximized rather than minimized the same request means "back to windowed size", so a game that needed nothing could be dropped out of fullscreen by the thing meant to help it. Windows is now asked whether that exact window is minimized, through a call Cheat Engine makes into itself, and nothing is sent to a window that is not. Each window is asked once, until Cheat Engine puts up another one.
- [New] Say when this Cheat Engine cannot be asked whether a window is minimized. Without that answer nothing is sent, because sending it anyway is what would take a healthy game out of fullscreen, so a game that keeps its sound and its controller and draws nothing is left that way. **Advanced** now names that as the reason, and the answer is checked against a window whose state is already known before it is used for anything.
- [Fixed] Find the game's window when something else has the same name. The handle came from asking the system for a window by its caption, which answers with the first one wearing it anywhere: on this hardware the game's own second window name belonged to a different program entirely. That handle is refused, correctly, but asking again returns the same one forever, so the game's real window by that name could never be reached. The list is now built by walking the system's own windows and keeping the ones the exact attached game owns, which needs no name at all.
- [Fixed] Stop closing Cheat Engine windows that hiding already deals with. Only the main window was recognised as one to leave alone, and Wine keeps a window in front after it has been hidden, so a Cheat Engine tool window or a window a table made for itself could be hidden, stay in front and then be closed a second later, cancelling whatever it was doing. Every window Cheat Engine assigns to itself is now identified the same way the main one is, and a window that cannot be told apart from one of them is left alone. The window nobody can reach by hiding, which is the one this was written for, is still taken down.
- [Fixed] Keep Cheat Engine running when the game's launcher hands off and exits. Whatever was running when the launch started is only startup context, and a game that starts through a launcher or a bootstrap process replaces that entire set of processes moments later. That was read as the game having exited, and the attached Cheat Engine and its table were stopped over a game that was still running. Where the session knows the game's own program, only looking for that program can end the launch now.
- [Fixed] Let the repair under **Advanced** repair the state it is offered for. A stop that could not prove Cheat Engine gone keeps that doubt, and the repair action refused while any such doubt was held, before performing the very check that settles it, so the compound failure it exists for was the one it could not act on. It now asks that question first, exactly as starting Cheat Engine does.
- [Fixed] Never delete a downloaded table while it is being imported. Staging is cleaned up half an hour after an acquisition was last touched, and the sweep runs from the panel's own polling, which did not know an import was in progress: an import started just before that boundary could have its own source file deleted underneath it, and it failed with whatever it was reading at that moment. An import in progress is no longer something to clean up, and the half hour resumes when it finishes.
- [Fixed] Say that a cheat's own script cannot be run, instead of failing the cheat. A cheat inside a script does not exist until that script has run, and when two entries in a table share that script's number nothing can name it. It was quietly left out, so the cheat was offered, saved and auto-loaded, and every attempt waited for something Cheat Engine never creates and then blamed the cheat. Such a cheat is no longer offered, and a saved one names the script that cannot be run.
- [Changed] Stop auto-loading a table's own "attach to the game" record. A table used before CE Decky recognised those records can still have one remembered as switched on, and switching it on re-opens the game by name, which can move Cheat Engine off the exact process it was given. It is still switchable by hand under **Scripts**, where you are there to see what it does.
- [Fixed] Report a save that already happened as saved. CE Decky treats an error from the backend as proof that nothing was written, and that was not true of the last step of writing a file: the new contents are already in place by then, and only their survival of a power cut is in doubt. A stored table selection could be reported as a failed activation, and a cheat Cheat Engine had already been told to switch could be reported as never sent, with a retry offered for it. Those two now say the outcome is unsettled and check, rather than saying nothing happened.
- [Fixed] Reconcile every write that selecting a table makes. Activation saves the profile, authorizes the table and stops the previously running Cheat Engine, and a lost reply from any of them was reported as the whole thing having failed, leaving the game without its table and the panel describing state the backend did not have. Each write is now confirmed against what was actually stored, and one that fails after another has been stored says so. Selecting a table also no longer records the same table twice.
- [Fixed] List a record with the scripts only when its script does nothing else. A record was treated as a table's own attach button whenever a line mentioned opening the process anywhere in it, which reads a line that opens the process and then does something else as if the something else were not there, and a `process(...)` written by the table's author counted as well. Either could take a real cheat out of the picker's default view. The line now has to be the attach call and nothing besides.
- [Changed] Say what to press when Cheat Engine will not start. The refusals reach you as a notification and nothing else, and "stop it before starting another Cheat Engine" states the rule rather than the move, on a panel where nothing looks to be running. Each one now names the press that clears it: Stop CE for a Cheat Engine still running, the game that holds it when another game does, and the repair under **Advanced** when the ownership record itself cannot be read.
- [Fixed] Stop refusing to start Cheat Engine because of one that already exited. When a stop could not prove the owned Cheat Engine gone, that doubt was kept forever: every later start was refused with "stop it before starting another Cheat Engine", for a process that was no longer there, and reloading the plugin was the only way out. The doubt belonged to that moment, so it is now re-asked, and a proven absence releases it.
- [Fixed] Give the game its picture back. Starting Cheat Engine takes the foreground from the game, and a game that loses the foreground minimizes itself: it keeps its sound and its controller and draws nothing at all, and taking Cheat Engine's window away does not undo it, because the game is minimized in its own bookkeeping and nothing outside it can say otherwise. The game is now asked to come back, and it does.
- [Changed] Ask only a game that was actually pushed aside. The request is sent while the game is not the window in front, which is the state it exists to undo, and it stops the moment the game is back. A game holding the foreground is never touched, because for a window that is maximized rather than minimized the same request means "back to windowed size", and a game that was fine would not stay fine.
- [Fixed] Take down a Cheat Engine window that hiding cannot reach. Cheat Engine sometimes asks a question in a window of its own, and such a window is not one of the forms CE Decky sweeps: on this hardware one sat over a running game unseen, and the game kept its sound and its controller and lost its picture entirely. Any window Cheat Engine holds in front is now dismissed, which Cheat Engine treats as cancelling the question.
- [New] Say what was dismissed. Answering a question on your behalf is not something to do quietly, so **Advanced** reports how many Cheat Engine windows were taken down and what the last one was called. If a table stops behaving right after one appears, the cancelled question is the first place to look.
- [Changed] List a table's own "attach to the game" button with the scripts instead of among the cheats. It changes nothing in the game: CE Decky has already attached to the exact process by then, and switching it on would re-open that process by name. It is recognised by what its script does and not by what it is called, so a cheat whose script merely starts with Lua is unaffected, and the **Scripts** switch still shows it.
- [Fixed] Say what a game running without Proton actually needs. A native Linux build runs under the Steam Linux Runtime, which is a compatibility tool but not a Proton one, and CE Decky reported that as no installed Proton matching, which sends you looking for a broken Proton install. There is no Windows process to attach to at all, and the answer is to force a Proton compatibility tool for that game in Steam, which installs its Windows build.
- [Fixed] Say why **Processes** cannot be pressed. It asks a running Cheat Engine which Windows processes it can see, so with none running there is nothing to ask - but the button simply greyed out, which reads as a broken button rather than a waiting one. **Advanced** now names the reason: no active table, no prepared session, Cheat Engine not running, or a session belonging to a different game or table.
- [Fixed] Stop reading the game's cheat state after the panel is closed. Refreshing live state walks every switchable cheat in the table, which for a large table is well over a hundred round-trips to Cheat Engine, and closing the panel left all of them running: CE Decky ignored the answers, but it kept asking, for seconds after there was nothing to show them in. Closing now stops the sweep. A change you asked for is still seen through to the end.
- [Changed] Never lose a table to a page CE Decky could not fully read. Everything a source says about a table, its version, size, author, date, notes and recorded origin, is description around the link that makes it obtainable: a described value that cannot be read is dropped, a row whose own identity is unusable is skipped alone, and a page that cannot be read at all costs that page rather than the provider and everything else it had already returned. The same holds after download: provenance that cannot be recorded never withholds a table whose bytes are verified and stored. Digest, host, archive, executable-content and consent checks are untouched and still fail closed.
- [New] Count what could not be read, per source, under **Advanced -> Debug**: unreadable pages, where nothing on that page can be downloaded, kept apart from partly read rows, where only description was lost. A source that quietly stops being readable otherwise looks like a game with no tables. One bounded line per source per search, with sample reasons, also goes to the log.
- [Fixed] Stop calling two different numbers the version. **Advanced** described the active table as `version 52`, which is the table format number Cheat Engine's own file declares, while search offered that same file as `v1.0.6`. The format number is now labelled as one.
- [New] Keep the release a table was downloaded as. An import records the version the provider advertised for those exact bytes, and **Advanced** shows it beside the table, so a file downloaded as `v1.0.6` still says so afterwards. A table imported before this, or from a source that advertised no version, simply shows none.
- [Fixed] Show which revision of a table each FearLess download is. One post carries every revision, all sharing a filename and the post's date, and the release is published as the uploader's own comment beside each attachment: an unmarked `1.0.6`, which the rule that keeps attachment sizes from being read as versions refused. That comment has its own place in the page, a size never appears in it, and the six revisions of the target's table now list as `v1.0.6` down to `v0.1.0` instead of six identical rows.
- [Changed] Stop repeating a source's own count back at the user. A provider that refuses anonymous downloads is already listed as `n/a` on the line of per-source counts under the search; the banner that said the same thing again sat between that line and the results, on every search.

## 0.9.5 — 2026-08-31

- [Fixed] Load the cheat table into Cheat Engine, instead of hoping Cheat Engine opens it. Cheat Engine opens a table named on its command line only once its main window is actually shown - and CE Decky deliberately never lets that window map, because a Cheat Engine window that appears takes the running game's audio and controller input with it. So a game could end up with Cheat Engine attached and an empty table: every cheat reported missing, none of them with a switch, and none of them able to be pinned to the panel. CE Decky now loads the exact reviewed table through Cheat Engine's own API, which needs no window at all, and the launch no longer mentions the table on the command line.
- [Fixed] Keep a Cheat Engine that does not expose `loadTable` from looking like a broken table: an imported installation that cannot be driven says exactly that, once.
- [New] Remember the tables that did not work, so the same file is not found, downloaded and reviewed all over again. A table is recorded when Cheat Engine runs one of its cheats and the cheat comes straight back off - which is what a table written for a different build of the game does - and when a download turns out not to be a usable table at all. The record is the table's exact contents, so it survives the file being renamed or served from a different post, and it survives a fresh search: bytes do not start working because the catalog was re-read.
- [Fixed] Record a refused cheat auto-load never asked for by name. Preparing a session adds the enclosing scripts a saved cheat needs, and for a table whose cheats all live inside one master script that script is exactly what fails - so the refusal that matters most was the one class not being recorded.
- [Fixed] Let the dialog act. It was raised from inside the operation that produced the refusal, which still held CE Decky's single-operation lock, so answering it quickly closed the dialog and had the answer refused as "another operation is still running" - nothing happened, and nothing said so. It now appears only once that lock is actually released, however long that takes, and is dropped rather than arriving over a panel that has since been closed.
- [Fixed] Make **Clear all** able to repair the record it offers to repair: it read the record before replacing it, so for the corrupt state that is the only reason to press it, it could not.
- [Fixed] Record a refused cheat from every press that can produce one, not only from **Apply**: the panel's own pinned toggle makes the same runtime change, and auto-load hits the refusal first of all - every launch, before the picker is ever opened.
- [Fixed] Actually run that dialog from the press that produces the refusal. It was wired to the pin write, which stores durable metadata and never activates anything, so no ordinary “switched it on and it came straight back off” ever reached it.
- [Changed] Only call the table unusable when a cheat would not switch **on**. A cheat that will not switch **off** is very likely still running in the game, and describing that as a bad table both misdescribes it and hides it.
- [Fixed] Do not report a table as marked when recording it failed - search would offer it right back - and tell an unreadable record apart from an empty one under Advanced, since nothing is being refused while it cannot be read.
- [Fixed] Switch auto-load off as a verified change when you stop using a refused table. Withdrawing the authorization never cleared it, and a swallowed failure left the table armed to start by itself the moment it was authorized again.
- [Fixed] Take Cheat Engine's windows down whenever the sweep cannot prove they are already down, instead of only when it can see one - an enumeration Cheat Engine does not support, or one that fails, used to leave a window over the game indefinitely.
- [New] Say it in front of everything else when a table does not work. Cheats are switched on from the configurator, so a message on the panel behind it is one nobody reads: there is now a dialog naming the cheat and what it usually means, a notification, and one press that stops using that table for this game - it withdraws the authorization, switches auto-load off and stops Cheat Engine, while leaving the table, its cheats and its saved values exactly as they were.
- [New] List them under **Advanced → Tables that did not work**, with the reason, the game, the version that game was on, and the day. Clearing one makes exactly that file offerable and importable again, which is what a game update usually calls for; Clear all empties the list.
- [Changed] Grey a table already recorded as not working wherever search advertises its contents, and refuse to import those exact bytes by any route - a provider download, an archive member, or a local file - naming the reason that was recorded.
- [Fixed] Keep Cheat Engine's windows down for the whole session, not only when it starts. CE Decky hid them once, from the moment Cheat Engine first shows its window - which is the moment that takes a running game's audio and controller input - but a window Cheat Engine mapped later stayed up, as an empty frame over the game with the gamepad overlay drawing a focus box around it. Any window Cheat Engine puts up is now taken back down within about a second, and **Advanced → Runtime** counts how often that happened, so a game that loses its sound can finally be attributed to something.
- [Changed] Say what happened when a cheat will not switch on. Cheat Engine running a cheat's script and having it come straight back off is the ordinary outcome for a table written against a different build of the game - and it was reported as "activation did not settle", which names neither the cheat nor anything a person can do. It now names the cheat and says what that normally means. Every other refusal keeps the reason Cheat Engine or the bridge gave.
- [Fixed] Keep reading the heartbeat of a Cheat Engine that was already running before this update. It still writes the diagnostics its own older bridge knows, and one retired diagnostic was enough to make CE Decky report the whole session as unreadable protocol state - which reads as corruption, hides the live cheats and disables the process list. Retired diagnostics are now accepted and ignored.
- [Fixed] Say when Cheat Engine could not open the table. That used to be indistinguishable from a table CE Decky cannot support; the panel now reports it, with Cheat Engine's own reason where there is one.
- [Fixed] Stop switching **Load last table & cheats** on for a table nobody switched a cheat on in. Opening the picker with nothing running, typing a value and pinning a row is configuring the table, not asking for it to run - and the value belongs to a cheat that is still off, which the runtime already declines to write. Only a cheat actually switched on now starts Cheat Engine with the game, and the picker says exactly that before Apply.
- [New] Offer the launcher a game ships beside the executable a table names, so a game whose Steam entry starts `SomeGame_Launcher.exe` no longer shows one choice when it has two. The table's own hint and the running game outrank it, so it is listed and marked as the launcher rather than picked over them; it is still the default when it is the only thing known, because for plenty of games the executable Steam starts is the game. A store client is not a candidate: it belongs to no game.
- [Changed] Wait inside the header. The spinner for a table being downloaded, and the one for a table's cheats being read, each had a row of their own directly under the header, which pushed every control below them down for the length of the wait and then let them jump back up. Both now sit in the spare width at the right of the header they belong to.

## 0.9.4 — 2026-08-30

- [New] Finish first-run setup even after Cheat Engine's download link moves. The link CE Decky ships is the only one anything official publishes, and it changes with every release. When it stops working, setup now reads the current one from cheatengine.org the same way the site's own download button does - and never runs anything it downloads to find it.
- [Security] Accept a rediscovered artifact only on evidence, never on where it was found: it has to be the exact reviewed release, byte for byte. The byte limits and host rules are the reviewed ones, each step's allowed host comes from what the previous pinned step named, and one ambiguous answer anywhere refuses the whole route. A failure to write the download locally never sends CE Decky to the website.
- [New] Tell a Cheat Engine release CE Decky simply cannot unpack apart from an artifact that cannot be trusted. A rediscovered download's Authenticode signature is checked against the exact publisher key CE Decky reviewed, with no certificate authority trusted in its place. It never authorizes an install - only the reviewed hash does - but it decides which refusal you are shown: a different release from Cheat Engine's own publisher says only the packaging is the problem and points at updating CE Decky or importing your own.
- [Changed] Show the Cheat Engine version the installed executable declares rather than the version this build was packaged against, so a newer release is never labelled as the older one.
- [New] Say where the installed Cheat Engine came from, under **Advanced → Cheat Engine installation**: which route found it, what vouched for it, the signer when a signature was what proved it, the artifact's hash and size, and the hosts involved. Rediscovery finishes silently, so this is where it is stated.
- [Limitation] Only the Cheat Engine release this build reviewed can be installed: the extractor reads that artifact's exact packaging and nothing else. If the download link starts serving a different one, setup says so and points at manual import; a future CE Decky release reviews the new artifact.
- [Changed] Explain what to do when no download route works. Instead of one line under the button, setup now says what failed, that nothing was installed, and how to finish by hand: install Cheat Engine on Windows, bring the folder or a .zip of it across, and import it from Advanced.

## 0.9.3 — 2026-08-30

- [Fixed] Publish a release again. The tag workflow still checksummed a source archive whose generator was removed, so the first stable tag would have failed after spending a full release validation getting there.
- [New] Delete CE Decky's data from **Advanced → Removal**, at a scope you choose: the search cache, staging and logs; that plus every game's saved setup; or everything, including your imported tables and the installed Cheat Engine. The choice states what it removes and how much, and the press that performs it asks once more in Steam's own dialog, naming what cannot be undone; neither the press that opens the choice nor the one that picks a scope deletes anything.
- [Limitation] Deleting the logs unlinks the file Decky's logger still holds open, so entries written before the plugin next reloads go nowhere. Everything after a reload is logged normally.
- [Security] Refuse to delete anything while work is still executing out of the tree being removed: a Cheat Engine process CE Decky owns, an installation that is running or has been reserved, or a download that still holds a staged file - including one that finished and is waiting for you to pick a table inside it or type its password, and one handed to the browser that has not been resolved yet. No new download can start while a deletion is running. Corrupt profile or session state does not refuse, because a full reset is how a user escapes it. Deletion never follows a symlink out of the plugin's own directories - not even to measure what it is about to remove - and never accepts a path from the caller, only one of the three named scopes.

## 0.9.2 — 2026-08-30

- [Security] Never treat another table's profile as proof this one's authorization was withdrawn. If the selected table changed while a lost reply was being reconciled, the withdrawal was reported as done while this table's own authorization stayed archived with it - and came back the moment it was selected again.
- [Fixed] Ask what was actually saved for the last two Advanced writes as well. Clearing a table's saved startup actions, and withdrawing its authorization to run, reported a lost reply as a failure after the profile had already taken the change - and the second of those invited the user to withdraw an authorization that was already gone.
- [Fixed] Stop saying a cheat selection "was not remembered" in the one case CE Decky cannot tell. Switching cheats off from Home or with `Disable all` prefixed a definite failure onto an outcome the same message then said it could not confirm.
- [Fixed] Ask what was actually saved for each setting separately. Switching cheats off, pinning one to Home, and switching Load last table & cheats used to report a lost reply as a failed change; and an Apply that saved the selection and was then refused the auto-load switch reported both as lost. The picker now names the part that is already saved.
- [Fixed] Stop a failed read of the live cheat state from reporting an attached, working session as a failed Auto-load. The panel says the live state could not be read and why, and the session, its startup and its saved cheats are unaffected.
- [Fixed] Keep every table reachable inside an archive imported before CE Decky recorded how many tables it held. Such a download is offered again and re-inspected rather than always reopening the one member already imported.
- [Fixed] Say that a password-protected .7z cannot be opened wherever one can be chosen, not only when it is opened from a local file. A download in which every table is one now ends as failed instead of waiting for a choice with no valid answer; a mixed archive keeps its other tables importable.
- [Fixed] Clean up the first-run self-test's Proton prefix after its processes are proven gone, so a prefix that finished being created while Cheat Engine was stopping is still cleaned up rather than left behind.
- [Fixed] Refuse to start a second Cheat Engine when ownership cannot be read at all, at the point one is actually started - the panel already refused on that evidence, and the spawn boundary did not.
- [Fixed] Treat a Proton prefix that has not finished being created as nothing to clean up, so cancelling the first-run self-test early no longer reports a cleanup failure after the process really did stop.
- [Security] Accept nothing for a table record that declares itself read-only while declaring no values, instead of offering it as a free-text field and accepting anything.
- [Fixed] Ask what was actually saved when a reply is lost, rather than reporting a saved selection as a failed one and offering to discard it. The same applies to a download whose import had already finished.
- [Fixed] Name every part of a failed Apply that already took effect - the running game, this table's saved configuration, or both - and ask before closing over it.
- [Fixed] Let a table with more cheats than can be controlled live still load, attach and auto-load; only the live controls become unavailable, and the panel says so instead of reporting a working session as failed.
- [Fixed] Keep a long list of saved cheats from being reported as unfinished while it is still being applied.
- [Fixed] Keep every table inside a multi-table archive reachable, instead of always reopening the first one that was imported from it.
- [Fixed] Say that a password-protected .7z cannot be opened rather than asking for a password that can never work. Password-protected .zip is unaffected.
- [Fixed] Roll back the startup action that failed, not just the ones before it. A value the table clamped was written before its read-back disagreed, so a failed Auto-load could report the game as restored while that record was still changed.
- [Fixed] Never delete the last good heartbeat for a replacement that has not landed: a failed rename left the session with no heartbeat at all, which reads as protocol corruption on a perfectly healthy bridge.
- [Fixed] Give Auto-load a deadline the bridge can actually meet. It allowed the whole startup less time than the bridge may legitimately spend on one asynchronous cheat, and then said the cheats had not been applied for a startup that went on to apply them.
- [Fixed] Say when a value was already saved even though the rest of Apply failed without reaching the game, instead of offering a Discard that cannot undo it.
- [Fixed] Budget the older per-record startup settings against the same exact plan as everything else, so one cannot be accepted and then make the next launch impossible.
- [Security] Refuse to attach to a game running a known anti-cheat launcher at the point Cheat Engine is actually started, not only in the panel. Nothing disables, hides from, or interferes with anti-cheat software.
- [Changed] Report a source that refuses anonymous downloads as unavailable and skip it, instead of offering to continue in a web browser.
- [Fixed] Try the archive password the source published beside the file before asking for one, so a completed download no longer ends at a password box nobody can fill - including an archive holding one encrypted table, where nothing was offered at all.
- [Fixed] Report an interrupted reinstall of the Cheat Engine release already installed as interrupted, rather than clearing it as if it had finished - and leave the panel usable afterwards instead of stuck behind a setup that had already stopped.
- [Fixed] Give a game whose runtime protocol state is corrupt a way out. Strict parsing of the mutable control and status files was correct, but those failures escaped the runtime status read while the session pointer still parsed, so repair refused ("the session is readable") and every path that needs runtime status failed. That state is now reported as its own repairable condition and the existing repair accepts it, after the same proof that no Cheat Engine CE Decky owns is running.
- [Fixed] Give an unreadable `profiles.json` a controller repair. A corrupt file degraded to an empty profile list so the panel stayed alive, then refused every per-game write - table choice, authorization, cheat selection, deletion, even Cheat Engine re-import - with no way to replace it. Advanced can now discard it; the unreadable file is kept as evidence and imported tables and Cheat Engine are untouched.
- [Fixed] Let an ordinary identity change be launched again. Switching table, changing the target process or re-importing Cheat Engine leaves a readable but stale session, and the attached launch refused it before reaching the fresh session it prepares itself - which made the advertised table switch permanently unlaunchable after the first session. Preparation now states the exact-SHA consent requirement itself, so self-healing cannot become a way around it.
- [Fixed] Stop a full disk from becoming durable protocol corruption. The bridge removed the last good status file before it knew the replacement had been written; write, flush, close and rename results are now all checked and a rejected staging file is removed rather than promoted.
- [Fixed] Give Cheat Engine ownership one answer the whole panel uses. Four different readings of the same launcher-wide rule could each report "clear" while the backend treated the state as owned, so Home offered install, import, forget and Start actions that were certain to be rejected and never named the game that actually held Cheat Engine. Ownership is now classified by state, never attributed to a game its snapshot was not fetched for, and an unreadable inventory is published as uncertainty instead of an empty list.
- [Fixed] Keep the owner and the Stop action visible when a second game starts or observation becomes ambiguous, and re-read launcher ownership while no game is selected, so an owned Cheat Engine that exits stops blocking setup without opening Advanced.
- [Fixed] Stop auto-load depending on the optional managed-install capability. Its failure is degraded everywhere else, and gating on it silently disabled the automatic workflow for a valid imported Cheat Engine while the manual start beside it still worked.
- [Fixed] Stop writing game memory for a cheat that was explicitly switched off. A configured value is kept for whenever the cheat is switched back on; it is no longer applied at startup, where for a child of a script that stays off it could not be resolved at all and failed the whole auto-load.
- [Fixed] Make `Disable all` remember only what it actually switched off. It marked every supported record as a deliberate choice, including records that were never on, enclosing scripts that are CE Decky's own machinery, and children that ceased to exist when their script went off - after which the next auto-load waited for MemoryRecords that could never appear and reported failure even though every cheat really was off.
- [Fixed] Budget the startup plan the session will actually carry. Persistence counted stored fields while the descriptor adds the enclosing scripts a remembered record needs, so a profile could be accepted as within budget and then make every launch impossible. The picker now asks the backend for that exact expanded plan before its first runtime command, which also counts configured values it could not see.
- [Changed] Say that configuring cheats with nothing running will also start Cheat Engine with the game. Apply still switches **Load last table & cheats** on, because with nothing running that is the only way the selection reaches Cheat Engine at all - but the picker now states it before the press instead of reporting it afterwards.
- [Fixed] Always allow auto-load to be switched off. A deleted or corrupted table left it armed with no way to disarm it, and it became executable again the moment the table was repaired.
- [Fixed] Recover from a lost first status read without remounting the panel, and stop an optional capability refresh from reporting a successful import, clear or repair as failed.
- [Fixed] Say why diagnostics could not be read instead of showing an empty Debug screen, and stop imported-table selection from leaking a rejected promise or acting twice on a fast second press.
- [Fixed] Stop reporting an unknown runtime outcome as a definite failure. The backend commits a command batch before it answers, so a lost receipt or a lost status read means the game may already have changed; those are now unknown outcomes that force reconciliation, while a genuine backend rejection stays a definite failure.
- [Fixed] Make a failed auto-load say whether the game was put back. Startup rollback was a sequence of unchecked assignments that never waited for an asynchronous script restore and never read anything back; it now follows the forward path's confirmation standard and reports whether it completed or left part of the table applied.
- [Fixed] Reconcile the cheat picker with the live session after a failed Apply, instead of continuing to show intent the game does not hold - and stop the close prompt implying that **Discard** restores anything already written or saved.
- [Fixed] Bound live control to a size a controller workflow can complete, separately from the table parser's own ceiling, and stop a multi-batch query as soon as the screen that owns it closes so it cannot race the next action.
- [Fixed] Terminate a provider download that is answered with a challenge instead of the file, rather than leaving it in a state with no way forward, and guarantee that no failure can leave an acquisition the panel will poll forever.
- [Fixed] Use the archive password the provider published beside the file, so a completed download no longer dead-ends at a password box the user has no way to fill. The hint stays backend-owned and is never shown, logged or stored; an explicit prompt is still the fallback.
- [Fixed] Recognize a table already imported from an archive instead of downloading it again, and stop treating a provider's artifact ID as content identity - a fresh revision was labelled Local and silently opened the older bytes.
- [Fixed] Keep an unresolvable running game visible instead of dropping it, so two running games can never look like one, and stop reporting "nothing is running" from an answer that could not be parsed.
- [Fixed] Show and allow cancelling a Cheat Engine launch that is still waiting for the bridge, and bound each status poll so one that never answers cannot freeze the panel.
- [Fixed] Remember that a game's exact process was seen alive, so a game that exits between two scans - or during a plugin reload - can still stop the Cheat Engine started for it.
- [Fixed] Stop treating a still-valid older Cheat Engine as proof that an install or reinstall completed; an interrupted setup is no longer reported as if it had succeeded.
- [Fixed] Use one Cheat Engine release per backend for both what the panel describes and what the installer verifies and registers.
- [Security] Refuse to attach Cheat Engine to a game observed running a known anti-cheat launcher. The observation already existed but was used only to keep that launcher out of target selection; it now reaches the review, manual start and auto-load decisions. Nothing disables, hides from, or interferes with anti-cheat software.
- [Fixed] Make Advanced state one ownership policy: actions that stop the running Cheat Engine say so before the press, and the ones the backend would reject while any Cheat Engine is owned are disabled with that reason.
- [Fixed] Render runtime values through a display-safe copy, so an authorized table cannot use invisible or bidirectional characters to reorder the line a value appears in. The exact value Cheat Engine receives is unchanged.

## 0.9.1 — 2026-08-29

- [Fixed] Keep every enclosing script out of the profile, not only the ones one Apply happened to switch on or release. A script that stays legitimately on - because a cheat inside it is on - is still not a choice anyone made, so a stale entry written by an older build survived until that cheat happened to be switched off.
- [Fixed] Switch a script off once the last cheat that needed it is off, and stop remembering one CE Decky switched on itself. A script exists to create the records inside it, so one left running keeps its patch in the game for no cheat at all - and, recorded as though the user had chosen it, switched itself back on in the next session with every cheat under it off. Nothing is lost by not storing it: auto-load already derives the scripts a remembered record needs from the table's own structure. A script the user switches on themselves is still remembered, and a chain of nested scripts is released in one pass.
- [Changed] Switch auto-load off when the last cheat for a table is switched off with nothing running, the mirror of switching it on when cheats are first configured there. Both directions now ask the same question, so an armed auto-load no longer starts Cheat Engine on the next launch for a table with nothing to apply. A stored value on a cheat that is off is kept for whenever it is switched back on.
- [Fixed] Stop a cheat's **More** from lying about a row it cannot change. An active cheat that takes a value keeps its editor open whatever that button does, so it read "More" over an already-open row and did nothing when pressed. It now reports the row's real state and is disabled for exactly as long as the reveal is forced; switching the cheat off releases it.
- [Changed] Give an open cheat row a heading that is a step lighter than the rows it reveals, so it reads as one card whether or not the controller is on it. Steam's focus colour was doing that on the focused row and nothing was doing it anywhere else.
- [Changed] Keep what a cheat's **More** reveals inside that cheat. Every Steam field is inset by 20px and its text input by nothing, so the value editor ran the full width of the block while the labels around it stayed indented, and the whole detail block sat flush with the row's own heading instead of under it. The block is now indented behind a rule with the input inset to match, one step down the type scale. The aside explaining that a value waits for its script is a short note rather than a labelled field the size of a control, and the record's own line no longer takes the control's kind as a lowercase heading.
- [Fixed] Let a game with cheats running actually exit. Cheat Engine has to run inside the game's own compatibility prefix to read its memory, which makes it a client of the same Wine session, so that session and the whole Proton chain above it survived the game for exactly as long as Cheat Engine did - and every one of those processes reports the game's own AppID. The owned Cheat Engine was waiting for a process set it was itself keeping alive, Steam was waiting for the same chain, and the console sat on its shutdown screen until the user forced the game to abort. An attached launch now also watches the exact process its session was prepared for, and stops when the game's own program is gone; supervision regained after a plugin reload uses the same rule. A scan that cannot prove absence never ends a launch, and a target never seen alive never stops one.
- [Changed] Keep a pinned cheat on the panel to two lines whatever its table calls it. A record named "Reduction % (100 = immune, 0 = no reduction)" wrapped to four lines and pushed the cheats under it off the screen; the name and its context are now one line each, and the row scrolls its own full text back and forth while it has controller focus, so nothing is hidden by the truncation. Its switch is narrower, and the live-state line above is tinted so it reads as the heading of the cheats beneath it.
- [Fixed] Keep a value set in the cheat picker. It reached the profile only through the state derived from the post-Apply re-read, so a record whose address does not exist yet, a rejected write or a failed activation persisted Cheat Engine's own answer - a zero or its `??` placeholder - over the number the user had just typed, and the next session restored that instead. The typed value is now this exact table's own configuration, committed before anything is written to Cheat Engine and applied over the reconciled live state at auto-load. Emptying the field drops the stored value rather than writing a blank.
- [Changed] Show the live answer wherever the running session can read the address and the stored value only where it cannot, so a saved number never paints over what the game actually holds.
- [Fixed] Wait boundedly for asynchronous Cheat Engine script activation before running dependent controls, and keep pinned rows visible with their reconciled live state when a mutation fails.
- [Fixed] Suppress Cheat Engine's taskbar-owner and normal windows while preserving CE initialization, so starting an attached table cannot leave the game without audio or controller confirmation input.

## 0.9.0 — 2026-08-29

- [Fixed] Keep an explicitly off dynamic child off when it was already absent before Apply; preflight and final verification now accept the same effective inactive state.
- [Fixed] Add a controller repair for malformed durable Cheat Engine ownership. Advanced quarantines the record only after a complete launcher-scope scan proves no CE Decky descriptor-bearing process can still be running; ambiguous evidence remains blocked.
- [Security] Validate the complete recovered private-runtime identity before live commands: strict manifest, aggregate directory key, exact paths, prepared executable SHA and current executable/bridge bytes. An unreadable packaged bridge now fails closed.
- [Fixed] Distinguish a dynamically absent MemoryRecord from target detachment and other bridge failures. Only typed `record_missing` is tolerated; **Disable all** no longer reports success or rewrites remembered state after an operational failure.
- [Security] Do not treat procfs permission denial as proof that an owned process is foreign. Same-user denied CE/Proton/Wine candidates retain ownership, while exact runtime metadata still lets a complete scan prove unrelated processes absent.
- [Changed] Remove the redundant source-ZIP generator and its release-QA stage. Release QA no longer writes `ce-decky-git-import-ready.zip` beside the worktree; the ordinary Git worktree remains the source authority and the installable plugin ZIP is unchanged.
- [Security] Prove an owned Cheat Engine is gone by exact descriptor in *durable recovery* too, not only in Stop. Recovery still classified the launch record by process group, so a reload could clear ownership of a Cheat Engine that had left that group and was still attached - after which a table switch started a second one beside it. The same scan now decides, and an unreadable one keeps ownership.
- [Fixed] Make that scan able to prove absence at all. An ordinary `/proc` pass always meets processes whose environment the kernel refuses, and treating every refusal as ambiguous meant Stop could never confirm on a real machine. A refusal now means "not ours", which is exact: Cheat Engine is started by this plugin as the Steam user and its own environment is always readable.
- [Fixed] Enable a nested cheat on a table that creates it. Apply preflighted every requested record, so the child that only the not-yet-enabled parent creates failed the query and Apply aborted before the parent activation was ever sent. Records an ancestor in the same Apply will create are now deferred, mutated after that ancestor is on, and waited for boundedly.
- [Fixed] Stop the remaining live paths from failing on a record that does not exist yet: `Disable all` preflight and its caller's re-query, the pinned-toggle re-query, and the post-reload hydration.
- [Fixed] Keep a deliberate "off" for a record destroyed by the parent that owned it. It had no state row to derive from, so the choice was dropped and a stale active preference could survive into the next session.
- [Fixed] Stop announcing auto-load success before startup finished. The bridge now publishes `startup_state` (`applied`, `pending`, `failed`), and auto-load waits for a terminal answer and names the exact record when startup failed. A bridge from before this contract is still accepted.
- [Security] Refuse runtime commands in the *backend* for a Cheat Engine running a bridge this build replaced, and check the whole runtime identity through its manifest rather than the bridge digest alone. It was a panel-only guard, and Decky retains overlapping frontend observers across a reload.
- [Fixed] Make "one Cheat Engine at a time" atomic. The check and the start were separate, so two launches for different games could both observe no owner and both publish one; the self-test re-checked only itself after its own preparation.
- [Security] Validate a runtime manifest before collecting it. Collection deleted any sibling that merely contained a file with the reserved manifest name; it now requires the manifest to parse and to name that exact directory.
- [Fixed] fsync the session directory after discarding a corrupt pointer, so a repair survives power loss like `retire_session` already did.
- [Fixed] Keep provider result identity attached to the results on screen, not to the text field. Editing the query moved damaged-artifact marks and the expired-authority retry onto a search that had not run.
- [Security] Gate a stable release tag on the shipped target-gate registry instead of a README sentence, and name the gates that are still open.
- [New] Switch back to any table this game already imported, from Home → **Imported**. Every table was retained by exact SHA all along, but the only routes back were a provider result that still mapped to it or the original file on disk, so a table whose source file moved became unreachable state.
- [New] Continue an anonymous-blocked provider in Steam's browser. The backend already built that handoff URL; nothing rendered it, so the panel said the source was unavailable and offered no next step - while the README described a handoff that did not exist.
- [Fixed] Reacquire a cached search result whose backend authority expired. The result cache survives a backend reload and the backend's own bounded snapshot map does not, so a normal-looking row failed only on click. The search is refreshed once and retried instead.
- [Security] Refuse live cheat control for a Cheat Engine still running the resident bridge from a previous CE Decky version. An attached launch survives a plugin update on purpose, and nothing in the live session identified its bridge, so the frontend could issue current commands to an old resident protocol. Ownership and Stop are unaffected; the launch record now carries the bridge identity.
- [Changed] Allow one attached Cheat Engine at a time, across every game and the self-test, and say which game holds it. The private runtime is one shared directory keyed by CE/tree/bridge identity, and Cheat Engine writes into its own installation while it runs, so whether a second game reused that tree or was refused depended on timing.
- [Security] Refuse a stable release tag while `README.md` still carries its pre-release notice. Metadata and a green QA run cannot prove the hardware behaviour the project itself marks unconfirmed, and a tag otherwise walked straight to a published release.
- [Fixed] Repair a corrupt Cheat Engine registration from the controller. Reading a malformed config already degraded gracefully, but every write path required that same config to load, so import, archive import and forget all failed on it - and managed install could download, verify and promote a Cheat Engine and then fail at registration, leaving a completed operation that kept the whole panel busy. Registering or forgetting Cheat Engine now replaces an unreadable registration instead of refusing.
- [Fixed] Repair unreadable session state from the controller. Runtime status, session preparation, retirement and profile deletion all began from the same strict read, so a malformed current-session pointer blocked that game indefinitely even with nothing running, and the only fix was deleting files from a terminal. Advanced now reports the exact condition and offers to discard the pointer, once the backend has proved no owned Cheat Engine can still refer to it. The session directory is kept as evidence.
- [Fixed] Follow the game that actually started. A manual choice made while nothing was running was kept even after a different game started, so Home went on showing, searching and configuring the game the user was not playing and auto-load looked dead for the one they were. A manual choice is now an override only while its own game is running.
- [Fixed] Block auto-load when the selected table file is gone. `Load table & start CE` already required the exact verified table; auto-load did not, so an externally deleted or corrupted table left it armed and every attempt failed in session preparation. It now names the repair instead.
- [Fixed] Keep the cheat picker's unsaved-change state until the profile write commits. Both Apply paths cleared it before the parent persisted, so a failed write looked saved: stopped configuration lost the choices on Back, and a live session could leave Cheat Engine changed while the next session restored the old state.
- [Fixed] Rank and label Wine/Proton helpers in Advanced → exact-PID attach, and name a session-only target override. Recovery offered `xalia.exe` and crash handlers as equally valid targets, and a successful retry to a different process left Home implying the profile target and the live one were the same - so the next session repeated the failed attachment. The override can now be saved as the game's target in one press.
- [Fixed] Gate Install, Reinstall, Import and Forget on whether *any* game owns a live Cheat Engine, which is what the backend enforces. They were gated on the selected game, so a user could be offered actions that could only fail, with nothing naming the game that actually held it.
- [Fixed] Auto-load a table whose cheats live inside a script. The resident bridge resolved every startup record before executing any action, so for exactly the tables that need an enclosing script switched on first, the child lookup failed, the parent was never activated, and nothing retried. Startup now resolves each record immediately before acting on it, waits a bounded time for one an enclosing script has yet to create, and is driven every tick instead of only on attach.
- [Fixed] Require a startup value to read back as written, the same rule live Apply uses. A table that clamped, coerced or rejected a saved value still reported auto-load successful.
- [Fixed] Switch nested cheats off deepest-first in Configure cheats, and on shallowest-first. Disabling a script destroys the records it created, so the previous unconditional parent-first order could destroy a record it was about to verify and report a successful runtime change as a failure - leaving the user's choices unsaved.
- [Fixed] Accept a record that ceased to exist because its own ancestor was deliberately switched off. `Disable all` and Apply both treated that expected end state as an error, so the durable profile kept remembering cheats the user had just switched off.
- [Fixed] Stop letting one unavailable record invalidate the whole live view. A child that its script has not created yet is an expected per-record state; it used to throw away Home's entire snapshot, hiding pinned controls and disabling `Disable all` while the bridge was healthy. Readable records now render, and the picker shows the rest as waiting for their script.
- [Fixed] Stop leaking a table snapshot on every launch, and collect superseded sessions. The controller prepared a session only to satisfy a precondition in the launch path, which prepared another, so each launch left a full exact-SHA snapshot of up to 32 MiB that Cheat Engine never opened - and retiring a session removed only the pointer, never its directory. The launch transaction now owns session creation, and a short evidence history is kept instead of one directory per launch.
- [Fixed] Collect private Cheat Engine runtimes for superseded identities. The runtime directory is keyed by executable, source-tree and bridge SHA, so an ordinary plugin update materialized a new sibling close to a full Cheat Engine tree and nothing ever removed the old one. Collection runs only while no owned Cheat Engine can be executing out of any of them.
- [Fixed] Prove an owned Cheat Engine is gone by its exact descriptor identity, not by its process group. Proton starts the launcher in its own POSIX session, but Wine clients can leave that group and one was observed doing so, so Stop could report success while a matching Cheat Engine was still attached - and switching table then started a second one beside it. Stop now signals the group, then every surviving process carrying the exact descriptor, and requires a negative proof from a complete process scan before releasing ownership. An unreadable scan keeps ownership instead of guessing.
- [Fixed] Stop treating CE Decky's own Cheat Engine as evidence that the game is running. An attached launch inherits the game's `SteamAppId` on purpose, so the general process observers answered "is this game running?" with CE Decky's own helper and a game that had exited kept looking alive. Ownership observation is unaffected and still finds it.
- [Fixed] Re-establish game-exit supervision for a Cheat Engine that survived a plugin reload. Recovery proved ownership but started no monitor, so a process that would normally stop itself when the game exits could run indefinitely while Home hid its Stop action. The launch record now carries the game-liveness baseline; records written by an earlier build still load and stay controllable.
- [Fixed] Stop a status read from rolling back a Cheat Engine registration. `get_status()` backfills a missing version label for a CE registered before this plugin read versions, and it saved the whole config snapshot it started from without the mutation lock, so an import, forget or managed registration committed on another worker thread in between was silently resurrected as the old identity. The label now commits only while the exact identity it measured is still the registered one.
- [Fixed] Retry auto-load after a transient startup failure. The first failure stored an attempt key that was never cleared, so a game whose target process appeared a moment late, or whose bridge had not finished its first heartbeat, never auto-loaded again until the panel was remounted. Three widening retries replace the latch.
- [Fixed] Never default the target process to Wine/Proton runtime noise. When filtering left no candidates the observation was handed back unfiltered, so opening Review during startup could default to a helper such as `xalia.exe`, persist it on **Use this table**, and attach later sessions to it. Review now says the game process has not appeared yet and manual entry stays as the override.
- [Fixed] Keep the panel usable when the optional managed setup status cannot be read. One failed capability RPC left the capability null, which disabled game selection, table search, Advanced and the runtime actions of an already valid imported Cheat Engine for the life of the panel. The failure is now shown with a Retry action and blocks only the managed install controls.
- [Fixed] Scope damaged-artifact retirement to the search snapshot that proved the bytes bad. Refreshing one game's search cleared the marks for every other game whose results were still cached, so switching back offered the damaged artifact again and cost another countdown and download.
- [Fixed] Treat an exact digest as stronger evidence than a provider artifact ID. A provider may replace the bytes behind one ID; a fresh result advertising a different SHA-256 was still labelled **Local** and opened the previously imported revision without any download. Local reuse now requires the exact advertised digest when the result has one.
- [Fixed] Report the FearLess index as fully refreshed only when every page carries a timestamp. A page cached before per-page freshness existed is already counted as due, so reporting the oldest timestamp available claimed a refresh that never covered it.
- [Changed] Correct the storage estimate in `README.md`. First use needs roughly 230 MB, not 130 MB: the private runtime copy CE Decky launches is close to another full Cheat Engine tree beside the installed one.
- [Changed] Remove the last text describing the deleted Steam launch-option subsystem: the launch module history, the `_uninstall()` comment, the removal-readiness help in Advanced, and a dead harness anchor left in `src/index.tsx`.
